# The Kerr–Newman Penrose Inequality for Axisymmetric Electrovacuum Exteriors

Coordinate `EN-117-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0534` | `PM-260.4` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Kerr-Newman-Penrose-Inequality-for-Axisymmetric-Electrovacuum-Exteriors-October-5-2026` | date 2026-10-05 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Kerr-Newman-Penrose-Inequality-for-Axisymmetric-Electrovacuum-Exteriors-October-5-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the sharp Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality\n\n$`\\displaystyle m^2\\ge \\frac{A}{16\\pi}+\\frac{Q^2}{2} +\\frac{\\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.`$\n\nHere $`Q^2=Q_e^2+Q_b^2`$, and <i>J</i> is the conserved total angular momentum, including its electromagnetic contribution. The result applies to smooth axisymmetric electrovacuum exteriors in the stated one-ended topological and decay class, with a connected outermost, outer-area-minimizing future marginally outer trapped boundary, Coulomb electromagnetic asymptotics, zero ADM momentum, and the explicitly assumed physical-area condition $`A\\ge4\\pi\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}`$. No maximality assumption is made. On the strict area branch, equality within this class characterizes the original data as an admissible spacelike exterior slice of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime, with the boundary mapped smoothly to a future-horizon cross-section or the bifurcation sphere.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the sharp Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality
> 
> $`\displaystyle m^2\ge \frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.`$
> 
> Here $`Q^2=Q_e^2+Q_b^2`$, and <i>J</i> is the conserved total angular momentum, including its electromagnetic contribution. The result applies to smooth axisymmetric electrovacuum exteriors in the stated one-ended topological and decay class, with a connected outermost, outer-area-minimizing future marginally outer trapped boundary, Coulomb electromagnetic asymptotics, zero ADM momentum, and the explicitly assumed physical-area condition $`A\ge4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}`$. No maximality assumption is made. On the strict area branch, equality within this class characterizes the original data as an admissible spacelike exterior slice of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime, with the boundary mapped smoothly to a future-horizon cross-section or the bifurcation sphere.

- **claim-penrose-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-m-main)): Under the hypotheses of this section, $$\label{m:penrose} \boxed{\displaystyle m^2\ge\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}A.}$$ If $A>4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$ and equality holds, the original exterior $(\Omega,g,K,E_{\mathrm f},B_{\mathrm f})$ admits a smooth spacelike embedding into the domain of outer communication together with the appropriate future horizon of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime with mass $m$, angular momentum $J$, and charges $Q_e,Q_b$, in the conventions above. The induced metric, second fundamental form with a consistent future normal, and induced fields agree with the given data. The end approaches the corresponding spatial infinity. The embedding and normal extend smoothly to $S$, which maps to a smooth future-horizon cross-section or to the bifurcation sphere.* *Conversely, every such Kerr–Newman exterior slice satisfying all the hypotheses and meeting that horizon attains equality. Nonconstant time graphs and nonzero second fundamental forms are permitted.
  - Explanation: Establishes the mass bound on the assumed physical area branch. Equality on the strict branch identifies all original exterior data with an admissible subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman slice, and the converse permits nonconstant time graphs.
- **claim-orbit-topology** (classification, [source](#stmt-a-topology)): The quotient of $\Omega$ by the circle action is a half annulus: its boundary consists of the compact image of $S$ and two noncompact axis faces, each joining an endpoint of that image to infinity. There are no exceptional orbits. The regular part of $\Omega$ is a trivial principal circle bundle over the interior of this quotient. It admits a global angular coordinate that agrees with the prescribed azimuth on a smaller asymptotic end and with smooth polar gauges near the axes.
  - Explanation: Identifies the orbit space and excludes exceptional orbits. The regular circle bundle admits an angular gauge compatible with both the asymptotic azimuth and smooth axes.
- **claim-corrected-angular-flux** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-angular-momentum)): There is a smooth invariant one-form $\alpha$ with $$\mathrm d\alpha=*_g B^\flat,\qquad \alpha=O_2(r^{-2}).$$ The flux $$\label{a:total-flux} \frac1{8\pi}\int_\Sigma(K-\tau g)(\nu,\eta)\,\mathrm dA_g +\frac1{4\pi}\int_\Sigma\alpha(\eta)g(E,\nu)\,\mathrm dA_g$$ is independent of the invariant enclosing surface $\Sigma$ and of the smooth invariant gauge for $\alpha$. It equals $J_{\rm ADM}$ at infinity, and hence equals the prescribed $J$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a decaying invariant magnetic primitive after duality rotation and proves conservation and gauge independence of the electromagnetically corrected angular flux.
- **claim-potential-axis-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-axis-regularity)): In a compact ordinary axis tube, let $y$ be signed transverse polar radius and let $x$ be a coordinate along the axis. The functions $$\psi-\psi_{\rm ax},\qquad\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}$$ are smooth even functions of $y$ of order $y^2$, while $$z+\psi_{\rm ax}\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}\psi-z_{\rm ax}$$ is smooth even of order $y^4$. These statements hold with smooth longitudinal derivatives and at the boundary poles in one-sided charts. On the asymptotic end, the potentials are bounded, and near either pole their differences from the adjacent axis values obey $$\label{a:end-regularity} \psi-\psi_{\rm ax}=O(\sin^2\vartheta),\qquad \chi=O(r^{-1}\sin^2\vartheta),\qquad z-z_{\rm ax}=O(\sin^2\vartheta).$$ The corrected central coordinate satisfies the stronger uniform estimate $$\label{a:end-central} \widetilde z:=z+\psi_{\rm ax}\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}\psi-z_{\rm ax} =O(\sin^4\vartheta).$$ The estimates have the differentiated versions obtained from Equation <a href="#a:potentials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:potentials">[a:potentials]</a>; in particular their rescalings to end annuli have uniform bounds through the orders used below.
  - Explanation: Controls potential parity and vanishing at ordinary axes, boundary poles and infinity. The corrected central coordinate has stronger polar vanishing than the uncorrected twist potential.
- **claim-polarized-source-identities** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-polarization)): The data $(g_0,k_0)$ extend smoothly through the axis, have the stated asymptotic decay, and have the same ADM energy as $(g,K)$. For every invariant surface, its area and future expansion are unchanged. Let $\mu_0,J_0$ be the constraint densities of $(g_0,k_0)$, with $J_0$ a covector. Write $\mathrm d\mathcal A=f_a\mathrm dA_h$ and set $s=(-B_e,E_e,-Xf_a/2)$. Then $$\label{a:constraints} 16\pi\mu_0=2\big(\|\mathcal D_X\|_{g_0}^2+\|s\|^2\big), \qquad 8\pi J_0=2s\cdot\mathcal D_X.$$ The source combinations on the right are smooth across the axis. On a signed axis tube $s_1,s_2$ are odd of order $y$, and $s_3$ is even of order $y^2$. In particular the polarized data satisfy the dominant energy condition.
  - Explanation: Polarization preserves ADM energy and invariant surface area and expansion. Exact source identities retain the removed components and imply the dominant energy condition.
- **claim-electrovacuum-wave-map-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-a-quotient-proposition)): For an invariant electrovacuum spacetime the axial potentials satisfy $$\label{a:quotient} \mathrm d\chi=-\iota_\eta F,\qquad \mathrm d\psi=\iota_\eta *_4F,\qquad \omega=-\tfrac12X^3*_H\mathrm d\mathbf A, \qquad \mathcal D_X(n)=s,$$ on every invariant spacelike slice, where $n$ is its horizontal future unit normal. With $\Phi=(u,\psi,\chi,z)$, the equations reduce to $$\label{a:wave} \mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_\gamma=2\Phi^*G, \qquad \Box_\gamma\Phi=0.$$ The second expression is the wave-map tension for the target $G$. Conversely, these reduced equations reconstruct locally an invariant Einstein–Maxwell metric and field, up to angular and electromagnetic gauge. Their slice data recover the original metric, tensor, and fields from the polarized data, the potentials, and their normal velocities.
  - Explanation: Reduces invariant electrovacuum spacetime equations to an Einstein–wave-map system and reconstructs the metric and field locally, including the slice tensor and normal map velocities.
- **claim-target-geodesic-convexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-a-target-geometry)): The target $(\mathbb R^4,G)$ is complete, simply connected, and has nonpositive curvature. In particular any two of its points are joined by a unique geodesic, and squared distance is jointly convex along pairs of geodesics.
  - Explanation: Completeness, simple connectivity and nonpositive curvature give unique connecting geodesics and joint convexity of squared target distance.
- **claim-subextremal-model-parameters** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-model-parameters)): For every $R>0$ with $R^2>\sqrt C$, define $$M=F_*(R),\qquad a=-J/M,\qquad b=\sqrt{M^2-a^2-Q^2}.$$ Then $$\label{a:parameter-identities} b=\frac{R^4-C}{4MR^2}>0,\qquad (M+b)^2+a^2=R^2,\qquad F_*'(R)=\frac bR.$$ The function $b(R)$ is strictly increasing and maps this branch onto $(0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: Relates model mass, rotation and horizon scale algebraically. The positive radial scale increases strictly throughout the open physical branch.
- **claim-model-map-boundary-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-model)): Let $\Phi_*$ be the map of Proposition <a href="#a:quotient-proposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:quotient-proposition">7</a> for Equation <a href="#a:kn-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:kn-metric">[a:kn-metric]</a>, with axis values Equation <a href="#a:axis-values" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:axis-values">[a:axis-values]</a>. Its ADM energy is $M$, its angular momentum in the present convention is $J=-aM$, its charges are $Q,0$, and its horizon area is $4\pi R^2$. It is independent of Killing time. Define $$w=\sinh\sigma\sin\vartheta, \qquad q_* =\tfrac12\log(W\sin^2\vartheta).$$ With Euclidean strip derivatives, it satisfies $$\label{a:model-map} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_G(w\,\mathrm d\Phi_*)=0,\qquad -\Delta q_*=\|\partial\Phi_*\|_G^2, \qquad q_*(0,\vartheta)=\log(R^2\sin\vartheta).$$ The canonical slice and its tensor and fields extend smoothly to the bifurcation sphere, including the ordinary axis poles. Its potentials have the regularity of Lemma <a href="#a:axis-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:axis-regularity">5</a>; their renormalized components and $u_*-\log\sin\vartheta$ have bounded derivatives on compact closed strip ranges.
  - Explanation: Verifies the comparison model's conserved quantities, weighted harmonic-map equation, logarithmic density equation and horizon boundary value, together with smooth canonical slice data.
- **claim-prescribed-capacity-cut** (construction, [source](#stmt-c-cut)): Suppose a meridional length element in the upper half-plane is $$\mathrm d\ell=B(v,\alpha)\abs{\mathrm d(v+i\alpha)}.$$ Besides the boundary puncture $Z=0$, allow finitely many punctures $a_1,\ldots,a_N\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$. Assume the following.* 1. *Away from the punctures and the two ends, $B/\sin\alpha$ has a positive continuous extension to the closed strip.* 2. *As $v\to-\infty$, this quotient converges uniformly to a positive continuous function $b_-(\alpha)$. As $v\to+\infty$, $$\frac{B(v,\alpha)}{c_+e^{2v}\sin\alpha}\longrightarrow1 \quad\hbox{uniformly},\qquad c_+>0.$$ At the sides, these statements concern the continuous quotients.* 3. *Near each $a_j$, put $t=-\log\abs{Z-a_j}$ and $\beta=\arg(Z-a_j)$. For sufficiently large $t$, the same length element has the form $$B_j(t,\beta)\abs{\mathrm d(-t+i\beta)},\qquad c_j\sin\beta\le B_j(t,\beta)\le C_j\sin\beta, \qquad 0<c_j\le C_j<\infty.$$* *If every crossing has length at least $L>0$, then, for every $k>0$ and $0<L'<L$, there is a holomorphic univalent map on $\abs{\xi}>1$, smooth with nonvanishing derivative on $\abs{\xi}=1$, such that $$f(\overline\xi)=\overline{f(\xi)},\qquad f(\xi)=k\xi+O(1)\quad(\xi\to\infty).$$ Its boundary is a smooth Jordan curve enclosing $0$, its upper semicircle maps to a cut avoiding all additional punctures, and, in the coordinates $\xi=e^{\sigma+i\vartheta}$, $$\label{c:density} \left.\frac{\mathrm d\ell}{\mathrm d\vartheta}\right\|_{\sigma=0} \ge \frac{L'}2\sin\vartheta, \qquad 0\le\vartheta\le\pi.$$ Additional punctures not enclosed by the cut are marked points on the real sides of the parametrized exterior. The constants used to construct $f$ may depend on $k$.
  - Explanation: Converts a global crossing-length lower bound into a pointwise boundary density bound on a smooth conformal cut with independently prescribed exterior capacity. Additional punctures are avoided by the cut.
- **claim-smooth-lower-crossing-weight** (construction, [source](#stmt-c-lower-weight)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#c:cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:cut">11</a>, for every $\ell<L$ there is a weight $$W(v,\alpha)=b_0(v,\alpha)\sin\alpha\le B(v,\alpha)$$ whose crossing lengths are at least $\ell$, where $b_0$ is smooth, has even reflection at both sides, satisfies $0<m_0\le b_0\le M_0<\infty$, and is independent of $v$ on each sufficiently far end. It is smooth across the locations of the additional punctures.
  - Explanation: Replaces the continuous weight by a smooth bounded lower weight with product ends and reflection parity while retaining any strictly smaller crossing lower bound.
- **claim-angular-calibration** (construction, [source](#stmt-c-angular)): For every $L'<L$ there are a lower weight $W$ as in Lemma <a href="#c:lower-weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:lower-weight">12</a>, a number $L_1>L'$, and a smooth angular coordinate $\Theta:\overline{\mathcal S}\to[0,\pi]$ such that $$\label{c:calibration} \varphi=\cos\Theta,\qquad \abs{\mathrm d\varphi}_{\mathrm{flat}} \le\frac{2}{L_1}W\le\frac{2}{L_1}B.$$ Here $\Theta=0,\pi$ on the respective sides, $\mathrm d\Theta$ never vanishes, $\Theta$ has ordinary odd reflection at both sides, and on each far end it is an increasing angular diffeomorphism independent of $v$. Moreover, $\Theta$ is joined to $\alpha$ by a smooth family of angular coordinates with these properties, product on uniform far ends; the estimate in Equation <a href="#c:calibration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="c:calibration">[c:calibration]</a> is needed only at the final member.
  - Explanation: Constructs an angular submersion whose cosine has the required weighted gradient bound. Reflection, product ends and an isotopy to the original angular coordinate are retained.
- **claim-exterior-analytic-boundary-solve** (construction, [source](#stmt-c-analytic)): Let $\Theta$ be an angular coordinate with the product-end, reflection, and isotopy properties of Lemma <a href="#c:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:angular">13</a>. For every $k>0$ there is a real-symmetric holomorphic function $U$ on $\abs{\xi}>1$, smooth up to the circle and with $U(\infty)=0$, such that $$\label{c:boundary-equation} \Theta\bigl(\log k+\Re U(e^{i\vartheta}), \vartheta+\Im U(e^{i\vartheta})\bigr)=\vartheta \qquad(\vartheta\in\mathbb R).$$ The map $f(\xi)=k\xi e^{U(\xi)}$ is univalent on the exterior, and its boundary is a smooth regular Jordan curve enclosing zero.
  - Explanation: Solves the nonlinear exterior analytic boundary problem at arbitrary positive capacity and proves that the resulting map is univalent with a smooth regular Jordan boundary.
- **claim-riemannian-logarithmic-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-r-comparison)): Let $\Omega_r$ be the closure of the component toward infinity obtained by removing finitely many smooth compact invariant obstacles from a rotation exterior as in the axial reduction. The omitted region includes the original inner side, and $\partial\Omega_r$ is nonempty. Suppose the following hold.* 1. *The polarized metric $g_r$ is smooth up to its inner faces, asymptotically flat of order $q_d>1/2$ through at least two derivatives, and has finite ADM energy $E_r$.* 2. *The potentials are restrictions of potentials on the original rotation exterior. Their constants on its two axis sides are $(-Q,0,-2J)$ and $(Q,0,2J)$, and they have the polar and end regularity of Lemma <a href="#a:axis-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:axis-regularity">5</a> and Equation <a href="#a:end-regularity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:end-regularity">[a:end-regularity]</a>.* 3. *One has $$\label{r:scalar-hypothesis} R_{g_r}\ge 2\|\mathcal D_{X_r}\|_{g_r}^2,\qquad R_{g_r}>0.$$ Each inner face has strictly negative mean curvature for the normal toward infinity. Alternatively, the inner boundary is already a smooth invariant stable minimal boundary.* 4. *Every meridional path in the retained region joining the two original axis sides has $\mathfrak h_r$-length at least $L>0$. The same assumption may be verified by a length-decreasing projection from any added collars to a reference meridian with that lower bound.* *For every $R$ on the open physical branch $R^2>\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, let $M=F_*(R)$ and $b>0$ be the model parameters in Lemma <a href="#a:model-parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:model-parameters">9</a>. Then $$\label{r:mass-bound} E_r\ge F_*(R)+\frac b2\log\frac{L}{2R^2}.$$ If the given inner boundary is a single stable minimal sphere, the strict inequality $R_{g_r}>0$ may be omitted.
  - Explanation: Bounds comparison ADM energy by the model mass and a logarithmic crossing-length correction. Strict scalar positivity is dispensable when the supplied inner boundary is a single stable minimal sphere.
- **claim-physical-branch-optimization** (bound, [source](#stmt-r-optimized)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#r:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="r:comparison">15</a>, if $L/2\ge\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, then $$\label{r:optimized-bound} E_r\ge F_*\!\left(\sqrt{L/2}\right).$$ In particular, with $L=A_0/(2\pi)$ this becomes $$E_r\ge\sqrt{\frac{A_0}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A_0}}.$$
  - Explanation: Optimizing the logarithmic comparison gives the model mass at the crossing-area scale. The closed endpoint follows by a numerical limit through strictly subextremal models.
- **claim-sourced-data-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-generalized)): Suppose a smooth polarized rotation exterior has one asymptotically flat end, a compact weakly future trapped boundary, and the topology and potential regularity of the axial construction. Assume $g-\delta=O_2(r^{-1})$, $k=O_1(r^{-3})$, $\|s\|+\|\mathcal D_X\|_g=O(r^{-2})$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\delta})$ for some $0<\delta<1$, and finite ADM energy. The potentials have the axis and end regularity in Lemma <a href="#a:axis-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:axis-regularity">5</a>. On each signed axis tube, $s_1,s_2$ are smooth odd functions of order $y$, and $s_3$ is smooth even of order $y^2$, with smooth longitudinal derivatives. For each row, the functions $\|D_i\|_g^2,s_i^2$ and the covector $s_iD_i$ extend smoothly to the three-dimensional exterior through the axis. The source expressions below use these extensions at axis points; neither an individual normalized row nor its ordinary norm is required to extend smoothly. Suppose, at every point and for every $w\in T\Omega$ with $\|w\|_g\le1$, that $$\label{d:sourced} 8\pi\bigl(\mu+J_{\rm con}(w)\bigr) \ge \|\mathcal D_X\|_g^2+\|s\|^2+2s\cdot\mathcal D_X(w).$$ Let $L>0$ be a lower bound for all crossing lengths in the meridional area metric of $g$. For each admissible subextremal model radius $R$, with $F_*(R)$ and $b=b(R)>0$ as in Lemma <a href="#a:model-parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:model-parameters">9</a>, $$\label{d:sourced-comparison} E_g\ge F_*(R)+\frac b2\log\frac{L}{2R^2}.$$
  - Explanation: Extends the comparison to polarized sourced data with a pointwise unit-ball residual inequality and smooth quadratic source combinations across the axis. The local momentum density remains present.
- **claim-neutral-deformation-prescribed-loss** (construction, [source](#stmt-d-deformation)): Let $(\Omega,g,k)$ be a smooth polarized one-ended exterior, complete with its compact nonempty boundary included. Assume the boundary is strictly future trapped, $k$ has compact support, $g-\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every finite $j$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\delta})$ for some $0<\delta<1$, and $g$ has finite ADM energy. Let $\mathfrak m>0$ be a smooth invariant function such that $$\mathfrak m\le8\pi(\mu-\|J_{\rm con}\|_g),\qquad \inf_C\mathfrak m>0\quad\text{for every compact }C\subset\overline\Omega, \qquad \mathfrak m\ge c r^{-3-\delta}\quad\text{on the end}$$ for some $c>0$. There is an invariant reduced exterior $\Omega_{\rm red}\subset\Omega$, obtained by removing full trapped regions and retaining the component toward infinity, with smooth nonempty compact boundary, for which the following holds. For every sufficiently small fixed $\varepsilon>0$, and all sufficiently large integers $n$ along a sequence, there are smooth invariant functions $f_n,t_n$ and positive smooth profiles $l_n(t)$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \label{d:deformed-metrics} \bar g_n=g+l_n(t_n)^2\mathrm df_n^2,\qquad \hat g_n=e^{4t_n}\bar g_n,\qquad w_n=\frac{l_n(t_n)\nabla f_n}{\sqrt{1+l_n(t_n)^2\|\mathrm df_n\|_g^2}}, \qquad t_n\ge-\varepsilon,\\ \frac12e^{4t_n}R_{\hat g_n} \ge8\pi\bigl(\mu+J_{\rm con}(w_n)\bigr)-\frac{\mathfrak m}{2}. \label{d:deformation-curvature} \end{gathered}$$ The inner boundary has strictly negative mean curvature in $\hat g_n$ for its normal toward infinity. The metric $\hat g_n$ is complete with boundary, is asymptotically flat of order one with at least two controlled derivatives, and has finite energy satisfying $$\label{d:deformation-energy} E_{\hat g_n}\le E_g+o_n(1).$$ Here the reduced domain and its collars are fixed before $\varepsilon$ and $n$. For each fixed $n$ the functions are obtained by exhaustion of finite outer truncations; the error in Equation <a href="#d:deformation-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:deformation-energy">[d:deformation-energy]</a> tends to zero as $n\to\infty$ after that exhaustion, with the prepared data and $\varepsilon$ fixed.
  - Explanation: Produces graph and conformal deformations on a fixed reduced exterior, with a prescribed scalar-curvature loss, negative inner mean curvature and asymptotically nonincreasing energy. Exhaustion precedes the deformation-parameter limit.
- **claim-numerical-penrose-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-numerical)): For the data of Theorem <a href="#m:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:main">2</a>, $$m^2\ge\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.$$ More generally, in the sourced class of Proposition <a href="#d:generalized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:generalized">17</a>, if $L/2>\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, then $E_g\ge F_*(\sqrt{L/2})$.
  - Explanation: Derives the original-data mass inequality on the closed physical branch and the optimized energy inequality for the enlarged sourced class on its strict branch.
- **claim-enclosure-from-exterior-supports** (construction, [source](#stmt-d-weak-support)): Work in a connected compact barrier truncation as above. Let $D$ be a nonempty compact closed set, disjoint from the designated outer faces and containing a collar of any required inner face. Suppose that every smooth exterior support at an interior frontier point of $D$ has expansion at most $c$ for a smooth tensor $Q$. An exterior support means a smooth regular level through the point that contains $D$ locally on its inner side. Then, for every $c'>c$ for which the outer barriers remain strict, $D$ is contained in a smooth bounding region with outward expansion at most $c'$.
  - Explanation: Turns a weak support expansion bound for a compact closed set into a smooth enclosing region at any slightly larger threshold compatible with the outer barriers.
- **claim-deformation-curvature-square-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-d-identities)): For $\bar g=g+l^2\mathrm df^2$ and $\hat g=e^{4t}\bar g$, and for the actual trace $F=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{A_\chi}S_1$, one has $$\label{d:curvature} \frac12e^{4t}R_{\hat g} =8\pi\bigl(\mu+J_0(w)\bigr)+\mathcal T+w(F)-F\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}K -u^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}V,$$ where $$\begin{aligned} \label{d:quadratic} \mathcal T={}&\frac12\|T^{\rm tf}\|^2 +(M+pa\nabla_\perp t)\cdot(M+(p+2)a\nabla_\perp t) +4(p+1)\|\mathrm dt\|_{A_d}^2\notag\\ &+(\chi-\chi^2/4)q^2+2(p+1)\chi qa\,\partial_et -\frac\chi2Fq+\frac34F^2. \end{aligned}$$ It has the square completion $$\begin{aligned} \label{d:squares} \mathcal T={}&\frac12\|T^{\rm tf}\|^2 +\|M+(p+1)a\nabla_\perp t\|^2 +[4(p+1)-v]\|\nabla_\perp t\|^2\notag\\ &+4(p+1)d\left(\partial_et+\frac{\chi aq}{4d}\right)^2 +\frac34\left(F-\frac{\chi q}{3}\right)^2 +\frac{4d(9-d)}{3(4+pv)^2}q^2. \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $$\label{d:coercivity} \|T\|^2+\|M\|^2+dq^2+F^2+\|\mathrm dt\|_{A_d}^2 \le\Pi_n\mathcal T.$$ Here and below $\Pi_n$ denotes a positive polynomial bound in $n$; its coefficients may depend on the prepared data, fixed collars and cutoff profiles, but not on the truncation or the solution. Different occurrences may denote different such bounds. If $\mathrm dt=0$, then, with $Q_f=\|T^{\rm tf}\|^2+\|M\|^2+\chi q^2+F^2$, $$\label{d:floor-norm} \tfrac12Q_f\le\mathcal T\le Q_f.$$ At any boundary face on which $f$ is constant, $$\label{d:boundary-identity} \frac{V_\nu}{u} =d(H+4\partial_\nu t)-H+w_\nu(F-P_B),\qquad H_{\hat g}=e^{-2t}\sqrt d\,(H+4\partial_\nu t).$$
  - Explanation: Gives the exact scalar-curvature and boundary identities, a nonnegative square completion and polynomial coercivity. These identities isolate the divergence flux and the source loss used by the deformation.
- **claim-floor-contact-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-d-floor)): Choose $\delta_0>0$ sufficiently small, then $C_n$ sufficiently large with polynomial growth. On every sufficiently large finite truncation, no smooth solution of any homotopy stage satisfying $t\ge-\varepsilon$ can attain $t=-\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Excludes contact with the lower conformal floor throughout the homotopy after the source coefficients are chosen in the prescribed order.
- **claim-height-separation-full-regions** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-height-separation)): In the first two homotopy stages, for every compact $C_*\subset\overline\Omega_{\rm red}$ disjoint from the selected full black region, there are $\eta'>0$ and $n_0$ such that $h>b_-+\eta'$ on $C_*$ for $n\ge n_0$ and all sufficiently large outer truncations. The analogous statement for a compact set disjoint from the selected full white region is $h<b_+-\eta'$. These constants are uniform over the first two stages.
  - Explanation: Obtains strict height gaps on compact sets disjoint from the corresponding full trapped region, uniformly over the initial homotopy stages.
- **claim-collar-normal-slope-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-collars)): For all sufficiently large $n$ and sufficiently large outer truncations, solutions in the first two homotopy stages satisfy, with constants $c,C'>0$ independent of $n,R$ and the stage parameter, $$\label{d:collar-slopes} \frac c{\tau_0}\le\partial_\nu f\le\frac{C'}{\tau_0}\quad(B_b),\qquad \frac c{\tau_0}\le-\partial_\nu f\le\frac{C'}{\tau_0}\quad(B_w).$$ They also satisfy $b_-\le h\le b_+$ on any fixed compact core needed in Equation <a href="#d:offset-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:offset-margin">[d:offset-margin]</a>. In the third stage one has $\|\partial_\nu f\|\le C'/\tau_0$. These conclusions need no upper bound for $Z$.
  - Explanation: Controls the signed normal graph slopes on the trapped collars and confines the rescaled height on the necessary compact core without first assuming a ceiling for the conformal variable.
- **claim-weighted-collar-traces** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-weighted-trace)): In the first two homotopy stages, for every nonnegative smooth $\varphi$ one has $$\begin{aligned} \int_B L\varphi &\le \Pi_n\int_{\mathrm{coll}}u \bigl[(1+\sqrt{\mathcal T})\varphi+ \|\mathrm d\varphi\|_{A_\chi}\bigr], \label{e:weighted-trace-eq}\\ \int_B \varphi &\le \Pi_n\int_{\mathrm{coll}}\sqrt D \bigl[(1+\sqrt{\mathcal T})\varphi+ \|\mathrm d\varphi\|_{A_\chi}\bigr]. \label{e:unweighted-trace} \end{aligned}$$ The integrands on the right include fixed collar cutoffs. In the first stage, moreover, $$\label{e:small-boundary-flux} \|(V_\lambda)_\nu\|\le Cud \le C\frac{\tau_0}{\ell}L.$$ None of these estimates requires an upper bound for $Z$.
  - Explanation: Controls weighted and unweighted boundary traces by collar integrals and gives a small first-stage boundary flux. No upper bound for the conformal variable is required.
- **claim-homotopy-ceiling** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-ceiling)): For all sufficiently large $n$, and then all sufficiently large truncations $\Omega_R$, every smooth homotopy solution with $t\ge-\varepsilon$ satisfies $$\|Z\|+\|t\|+\|f\|+\|\nabla f\|\le C(n).$$ The constants are uniform over the four stages and $R\ge R_{\min}(n)$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the graph height, its gradient and the conformal variables at fixed deformation parameter, uniformly in sufficiently large truncations and throughout the homotopy.
- **claim-local-gradient-hessian-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-gradient)): Consider three-dimensional coordinate patches with a fixed positive lower bound for their size, uniform ellipticity and smooth bounds for their metrics, and, when present, uniform smooth geometry of one boundary face. Let $z$ be a smooth solution, including up to that face, of $$\label{g:axial-equation} A_\chi:\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}z=G_0,\qquad A_\chi=I-(1-\chi)e\otimes e,\qquad e=\frac{\nabla z}{\|\nabla z\|},\qquad 0<\chi_0\leq\chi\leq1.$$ At a zero of $\nabla z$ any measurable unit axis for which the equation holds is allowed. Assume $\|\nabla z\|\leq L_0$ and $\|G_0\|\leq Q_0$; on a boundary face assume that $z$ is constant. Only the displayed measurable bounds on $\chi$ and $G_0$ are used. There are $\alpha\in(0,1)$, $C<\infty$, and a uniform smaller patch size, depending on $\chi_0$ and the geometric bounds, such that $$\label{g:gradient-estimates} \\|D_xz\\|_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq C(L_0+Q_0),\qquad \int_{B_r}\|\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}z\|^2\,\mathrm dV_g \leq C(L_0+Q_0)^2r^{1+2\alpha}.$$ The first norm is on the smaller patch. The second estimate holds for all sufficiently small balls centered in it, with intersection with the domain understood at a boundary. The same estimate holds for $D_x^2z$ and coordinate volume. These are a priori estimates for the stated smooth solutions, with constants independent of their higher derivatives.
  - Explanation: Derives gradient Hölder control and local Hessian-square growth for smooth solutions of an axial elliptic equation using only measurable coefficient and forcing bounds. Constant Dirichlet faces are included.
- **claim-fixed-parameter-classical-bootstrap** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-regularity)): Fix the prepared data, the selected reduced domain and collars, and all deformation parameters, including $n$ and $\varepsilon$. Consider smooth solutions of the homotopy equations on sufficiently large finite truncations, on the side $t\geq-\varepsilon$ of the floor. The bounds for $f,Z,t,\|\nabla f\|$ established above imply uniform local classical estimates, including at the inner and outer boundary faces, through each prescribed finite order. Constants are uniform over the homotopy and the truncations and may depend arbitrarily on the fixed deformation parameters. In particular they are not asserted to be uniform as $n\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Bootstraps the established variable bounds to arbitrary prescribed finite classical order, including boundary faces. Constants may depend arbitrarily on fixed deformation parameters.
- **claim-floor-independent-trace-solve** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-trace-solve)): For every invariant $Z\in C^{1,b_{\rm reg}}(\overline\Omega_R)$ with outer value zero, the trace equation at the current homotopy stage has a unique solution with its assigned Dirichlet data. The solution operator is continuous into $C^{2,b_{\rm reg}}$, also in the homotopy parameter, and maps bounded input sets to bounded subsets of $C^{3,b_{\rm reg}}$. No lower bound for the implicitly defined $t$ is required.
  - Explanation: Solves the scalar trace Dirichlet equation uniquely for bounded invariant input without assuming the conformal floor. The solution operator has the stated continuity and regularizing properties.
- **claim-reduced-exterior-physical-solution** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-existence)): At each sufficiently large fixed $n$ there is a smooth invariant physical solution of <a href="#d:trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:trace">[d:trace]</a>–<a href="#d:source" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="d:source">[d:source]</a> on the reduced exterior, with its prescribed inner conditions and $t\ge-\varepsilon$. It is the local smooth limit of solutions on expanding truncations.
  - Explanation: Obtains a smooth invariant physical solution on the reduced exterior by solving on finite truncations and extracting a local smooth exhaustion limit.
- **claim-deformed-end-energy-flux** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-asymptotics)): At fixed $n$, the solution of Proposition <a href="#e:existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="e:existence">30</a> has exponentially decaying $f$, with derivatives through every required finite order, and $$Z,t=O_2(r^{-1}).$$ In particular $\hat g=e^{4t}(g+l^2\mathrm df^2)$ is asymptotically flat of order one. Its energy is $$\label{e:energy-flux} E_{\hat g}=E_g-\frac{\mathfrak F_n}{8\pi},\qquad \mathfrak F_n=\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R}V_\nu =\int_{\Omega_{\rm red}}u\Xi+\int_BV_\nu.$$
  - Explanation: Proves exponential decay of the graph function, asymptotic decay of the conformal variables and an exact ADM energy change expressed by the bulk and inner-boundary flux.
- **claim-vanishing-negative-energy-flux** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-flux)): With the prepared data and $\varepsilon>0$ fixed, $$\mathfrak F_n\ge-o_n(1),\qquad E_{\hat g}\le E_g+o_n(1) \quad\text{as }n\to\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Controls the negative part of the total deformation flux in the deformation-parameter limit, yielding the required upper bound on deformed ADM energy.
- **claim-equality-stationary-adjoint** (construction, [source](#stmt-j-adjoint)): There are smooth invariant fields $N,Y$ on the polarized exterior, with $Y$ meridional, such that $$\label{j:causal} N\geq \|Y\|_g,\qquad N>0\ \text{in }\operatorname{int}\Omega,\qquad \operatorname{sym}\nabla Y=-Nk.$$ For every $q'\in(1/2,1)$, their asymptotics are $$\label{j:asymptotic} N=1+O_2(r^{-q'}),\qquad Y=O_2(r^{-2}).$$ At the inner boundary, with $\nu$ directed into the exterior, $$\label{j:boundary} Y=N\nu,\qquad \partial_\nu N+k(Y,\nu)=\kappa.$$ Furthermore, either $N\|_S>0$ everywhere or $N\|_S\equiv0$.* *On regular orbits in the interior, define $$\label{j:stationary-metric} H_3=-N^2\mathrm dt_0^2+ h_{ij}(\mathrm dx^i+Y^i\mathrm dt_0)(\mathrm dx^j+Y^j\mathrm dt_0), \qquad \gamma=X^2H_3.$$ Then $(\gamma,\Phi)$, with $\partial_{t_0}\Phi=0$, solves the full Einstein–wave-map system of Equation <a href="#a:wave" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:wave">[a:wave]</a>. For the future $H_3$-unit normal $n$ to $t_0=0$, the induced horizontal tensor is $k\|_h$, and the map velocities are $$\mathrm d\log X(n)=k(e,e),\qquad \mathcal D_X(n)=s.$$
  - Explanation: At strict-branch equality, constructs a causal lapse and meridional shift with normalized end behavior and horizon boundary conditions. Their stationary quotient solves the full Einstein–wave-map system and recovers the slice velocities.
- **claim-minimum-area-envelope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-area)): The infimum defining $a(\widetilde g)$ is attained by an invariant filled inner set. The family $\mathcal T_0$ of invariant minimizers at $g$, viewed through their sets modulo null sets and their area and tangent-plane measures, is compact. At the equality data, $$a(g)=A.$$ For any of the smooth metric paths $\widetilde g_d$ used below, $$\label{j:envelope} \left.\frac{\mathrm d}{\mathrm dd}\right\|_{0+}a(\widetilde g_d) =\min_{T\in\mathcal T_0} A'_T,\qquad A'_T=\frac12\int_{\partial T}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\partial T}\dot g\,\mathrm dA_g.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes attainment and compactness for invariant minimum-area enclosures and identifies the one-sided derivative as the smallest area variation among background minimizers.
- **claim-positive-variational-multipliers** (construction, [source](#stmt-j-multipliers)): There are a probability measure $\omega$ on $\mathcal T_0$, a nonnegative finite measure $\lambda_S$ on $S$, a nonnegative locally finite measure $\Lambda$ on $\mathcal B$, and $z_0\geq0$, such that every variation in the space just described satisfies $$\label{j:multiplier-identity} 16\pi E'_g-c\int_{\mathcal T_0} A'_T\,\mathrm d\omega(T) =\int_{\mathcal B}C'\,\mathrm d\Lambda -\int_S\theta_+'\mathrm d\lambda_S+z_0a_{\rm dir}.$$ Here $a_{\rm dir}$ is the coefficient of the strict direction. The measures can be chosen invariant under rotations and azimuthal reflection.
  - Explanation: Separates the feasible variation space from a positive cone to obtain nonnegative constraint and boundary measures and a probability measure on the minimizing family.
- **claim-minimizer-measure-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-concentration)): The probability measure in Equation <a href="#j:multiplier-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="j:multiplier-identity">[j:multiplier-identity]</a> is concentrated on the cut $S$.
  - Explanation: Concentrates the minimizing-family probability measure on the original boundary by combining exact local slice variations, minimizing-frontier regularity and outermostness.
- **claim-adjoint-moment-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-regularity)): The interior moment distributions are smooth. Their first jets satisfy a homogeneous linear first-order system with coefficients smooth through the axis and one-sided smooth up to $S$. They extend smoothly to $S$, and the moment measures have no part supported on $S$.
  - Explanation: Upgrades the moment measures to smooth interior lapse and shift, prolongs their equations to a first-jet system and removes any separate boundary-supported moment measure.
- **claim-adjoint-end-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-end)): For the adjoint fields above, there are constants $N_\infty,Y_\infty$ such that, for every $q'\in(1/2,1)$, $$(N,Y)=(N_\infty,Y_\infty)+O_2(r^{-q'}).$$ The multiplier identity gives $N_\infty=1$, and positive ADM energy gives $Y_\infty=0$. In fact $Y=O_2(r^{-2})$.* *More generally, the elimination of $Y_\infty$ applies to any pair with these constant asymptotics that satisfies $\operatorname{sym}\nabla Y=-Nk$, provided $k=O_1(r^{-3})$, $N$ is bounded, the scalar linearization $L(g-\delta)=O(r^{-4})$, and the ADM energy is positive.
  - Explanation: Normalizes the asymptotic lapse and eliminates the limiting spatial translation using positive ADM energy. The final assertion isolates the hypotheses needed for that translation argument.
- **claim-boundary-lapse-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-j-dichotomy)): Either $N\|_S>0$ everywhere or $N\|_S\equiv0$.
  - Explanation: The boundary lapse is either positive everywhere or vanishes identically; mixed boundary behavior is excluded by the adjoint stability equation and outer area minimization.
- **claim-global-static-quotient** (construction, [source](#stmt-k-timelike)): The function $\mu_*$ is positive throughout the interior, including on the axes. There is a globally defined function $f$ on the open meridian such that $$\label{k:static} \mathrm df=-\frac{\alpha_Y}{\mu_*},\qquad H_3=-\lambda^2\mathrm dt^2+h_+,\qquad \lambda=\sqrt{\mu_*},\qquad h_+=h+\frac{\alpha_Y^2}{\mu_*},\qquad t=t_0+f.$$ The original horizontal slice is the graph $t=f$. Moreover $\rho=X\lambda$ is $h_+$-harmonic off the axes.
  - Explanation: Excludes an interior null set for the quotient Killing field and obtains a global static time transformation. The axial radius times static lapse is harmonic in the static meridional metric.
- **claim-static-boundary-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-k-static-boundary)): The static meridian has a smooth compact inner boundary, with $$h_+\|_{TS}=h\|_{TS},\qquad \lambda=0,\qquad \|\mathrm d\lambda\|_{h_+}=\kappa .$$ If $N\|_S=0$, then its boundary coordinates and $f$ are smooth in the original one-sided coordinates. If $N\|_S>0$, then $\lambda=\sqrt{\mu_*}$ is a smooth static boundary coordinate, the static metric has an isometric reflection across $\lambda=0$, and $$\label{k:log-time} f=-\frac{\log\mu_*}{2\kappa}+f_0$$ with $f_0$ smooth in the original coordinates up to $S$. These statements are compatible with ordinary axis regularity at the two poles.
  - Explanation: Establishes a smooth static inner boundary and its surface gravity. The positive-lapse case requires a logarithmic time term, while the zero-lapse case is smooth in the original boundary coordinate.
- **claim-static-rod-scale** (construction, [source](#stmt-k-rod-scale)): There are global static isothermal coordinates $(\sigma,\vartheta)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,\pi]$, with the inner boundary $\sigma=0$ and the two oriented axes $\vartheta=0,\pi$, in which $$\label{k:rho} h_+=P_+(\mathrm d\sigma^2+\mathrm d\vartheta^2),\qquad \rho=b\sinh\sigma\sin\vartheta .$$ The boundary area density is $$\label{k:area-density} X\sqrt{P_+}=\frac b\kappa\sin\vartheta =R_0^2\sin\vartheta\qquad(\sigma=0).$$ In particular, the scale $b$ is determined by the original area and the adjoint boundary condition.
  - Explanation: Constructs global static isothermal coordinates and fixes their asymptotic scale using the original boundary area and the adjoint surface gravity.
- **claim-kerr-newman-map-identification** (classification, [source](#stmt-k-identification)): In the coordinates of Lemma <a href="#k:rod-scale" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="k:rod-scale">42</a>, the stationary map and horizontal metric coincide with those of the Kerr–Newman model of parameters $M,a,Q$ from Lemma <a href="#a:model-parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:model-parameters">9</a>.
  - Explanation: Identifies both the stationary target map and the static horizontal metric with the comparison model after the horizon scale is fixed.
- **claim-original-data-spacetime-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-k-lift)): The map of the regular interior orbits into the model given by $$\label{k:embedding} t=f,\qquad (\sigma,\vartheta)=(\sigma,\vartheta)(x),\qquad \phi_{\mathrm{KN}}=\phi+\beta,\qquad \mathrm d\beta=\mathcal A+\omega_*\,\mathrm df$$ is well defined and induces all of the original data, after inverse constant duality rotation. It extends smoothly across the ordinary interior axes and approaches spatial infinity.
  - Explanation: Integrates the angular gauge correction and embeds the regular interior orbits as a time graph in the model. Metric, tensor and electromagnetic components are recovered, including those removed by polarization.
- **claim-smooth-future-horizon-attachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-k-horizon)): The map in Proposition <a href="#k:lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="k:lift">44</a> extends to a smooth spacelike embedding of the original exterior including $S$, with a smooth future unit normal. If $N\|_S=0$, its boundary is the bifurcation sphere. If $N\|_S>0$, its boundary is a smooth cross-section of the future horizon.
  - Explanation: Extends the original-data lift to a smooth spacelike embedding with future unit normal at the boundary. The boundary lapse determines whether the image is the bifurcation sphere or an open future-horizon cross-section.
- **claim-embedded-slice-end-parameters** (classification, [source](#stmt-k-end-identification)): Let an original, unpolarized data set satisfying the asymptotic and rest-frame hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#m:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:main">2</a> be realized as a spacelike slice approaching spatial infinity in a subextremal Kerr–Newman exterior with parameters $M,\widetilde a,\widetilde Q_e,\widetilde Q_b$. Then $$m=M,\qquad J^2=M^2\widetilde a^2,\qquad Q_e^2+Q_b^2=\widetilde Q_e^2+\widetilde Q_b^2 .$$ In a sufficiently distant stationary spatial chart the slice is a single graph $t=F(\bar x)$, with $\mathrm dF=O_1(\|\bar x\|^{-2})$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the intrinsic rest mass, squared angular momentum and total charge magnitude of an admissible embedded slice with its ambient subextremal model parameters. The distant end is a single graph with decaying gradient.
- **claim-no-partial-bifurcation-contact** (classification, [source](#stmt-k-horizon-cut-dichotomy)): A smooth spacelike cross-section of the closed future horizon bounding an exterior with $\theta_+=0$ and the all-cut outer area-minimizing property lies entirely on the open future horizon or is the bifurcation sphere.
  - Explanation: Uses the linear transverse null-expansion inequality and outer area minimization to exclude a horizon cross-section that touches the bifurcation sphere only partially.

```json
      "exact_statement": "The quotient of $\\Omega$ by the circle action is a half annulus: its boundary consists of the compact image of $S$ and two noncompact axis faces, each joining an endpoint of that image to infinity. It admits a global angular coordinate that agrees with the prescribed azimuth on a smaller asymptotic end and with smooth polar gauges near the axes.",
      "explanation": "Identifies the orbit space and excludes exceptional orbits. The regular circle bundle admits an angular gauge compatible with both the asymptotic azimuth and smooth axes.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-axis-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "In a compact ordinary axis tube, let $y$ be signed transverse polar radius and let $x$ be a coordinate along the axis. The functions $$\\psi-\\psi_{\\rm ax},\\qquad\\chi-\\chi_{\\rm ax}$$ are smooth even functions of $y$ of order $y^2$, while $$z+\\psi_{\\rm ax}\\chi-\\chi_{\\rm ax}\\psi-z_{\\rm ax}$$ is smooth even of order $y^4$. These statements hold with smooth longitudinal derivatives and at the boundary poles in one-sided charts. On the asymptotic end, the potentials are bounded, and near either pole their differences from the adjacent axis values obey $$\\label{a:end-regularity}\n \\psi-\\psi_{\\rm ax}=O(\\sin^2\\vartheta),\\qquad\n \\chi=O(r^{-1}\\sin^2\\vartheta),\\qquad\n z-z_{\\rm ax}=O(\\sin^2\\vartheta).$$ The corrected central coordinate satisfies the stronger uniform estimate $$\\label{a:end-central}\n \\widetilde z:=z+\\psi_{\\rm ax}\\chi-\\chi_{\\rm ax}\\psi-z_{\\rm ax}\n       =O(\\sin^4\\vartheta).$$ The estimates have the differentiated versions obtained from Equation <a href=\"#a:potentials\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:potentials\">[a:potentials]</a>; in particular their rescalings to end annuli have uniform bounds through the orders used below.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-axis-regularity",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-polarization",
        "exact_quote": "The data $(g_0,k_0)$ extend smoothly through the axis, have the stated asymptotic decay, and have the same ADM energy as $(g,K)$. For every invariant surface, its area and future expansion are unchanged. Let $\\mu_0,J_0$ be the constraint densities of $(g_0,k_0)$, with $J_0$ a covector. Write $\\mathrm d\\mathcal A=f_a\\mathrm dA_h$ and set $s=(-B_e,E_e,-Xf_a/2)$. Then $$\\label{a:constraints}\n 16\\pi\\mu_0=2\\big(|\\mathcal D_X|_{g_0}^2+|s|^2\\big),\n \\qquad\n 8\\pi J_0=2s\\cdot\\mathcal D_X.$$ The source combinations on the right are smooth across the axis.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-quotient-proposition",
        "exact_quote": "For an invariant electrovacuum spacetime the axial potentials satisfy $$\\label{a:quotient}\n \\mathrm d\\chi=-\\iota_\\eta F,\\qquad\n \\mathrm d\\psi=\\iota_\\eta *_4F,\\qquad\n \\omega=-\\tfrac12X^3*_H\\mathrm d\\mathbf A,\n \\qquad \\mathcal D_X(n)=s,$$ on every invariant spacelike slice, where $n$ is its horizontal future unit normal. With $\\Phi=(u,\\psi,\\chi,z)$, the equations reduce to $$\\label{a:wave}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}_\\gamma=2\\Phi^*G,\n \\qquad \\Box_\\gamma\\Phi=0.$$ The second expression is the wave-map tension for the target $G$. Conversely, these reduced equations reconstruct locally an invariant Einstein–Maxwell metric and field, up to angular and electromagnetic gauge. Their slice data recover the original metric, tensor, and fields from the polarized data, the potentials, and their normal velocities.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-model-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "For every $R>0$ with $R^2>\\sqrt C$, define $$M=F_*(R),\\qquad a=-J/M,\\qquad\n b=\\sqrt{M^2-a^2-Q^2}.$$ Then $$\\label{a:parameter-identities}\n b=\\frac{R^4-C}{4MR^2}>0,\\qquad\n (M+b)^2+a^2=R^2,\\qquad F_*'(R)=\\frac bR.$$ The function $b(R)$ is strictly increasing and maps this branch onto $(0,\\infty)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Phi_*$ be the map of Proposition <a href=\"#a:quotient-proposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:quotient-proposition\">7</a> for Equation <a href=\"#a:kn-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:kn-metric\">[a:kn-metric]</a>, with axis values Equation <a href=\"#a:axis-values\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-values\">[a:axis-values]</a>. Its ADM energy is $M$, its angular momentum in the present convention is $J=-aM$, its charges are $Q,0$, and its horizon area is $4\\pi R^2$. It is independent of Killing time. Define $$w=\\sinh\\sigma\\sin\\vartheta,\n \\qquad q_* =\\tfrac12\\log(W\\sin^2\\vartheta).$$ With Euclidean strip derivatives, it satisfies $$\\label{a:model-map}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_G(w\\,\\mathrm d\\Phi_*)=0,\\qquad\n -\\Delta q_*=|\\partial\\Phi_*|_G^2,\n \\qquad q_*(0,\\vartheta)=\\log(R^2\\sin\\vartheta).$$ The canonical slice and its tensor and fields extend smoothly to the bifurcation sphere, including the ordinary axis poles. Its potentials have the regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a>; their renormalized components and $u_*-\\log\\sin\\vartheta$ have bounded derivatives on compact closed strip ranges.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-a-model",

Define $$w=\\sinh\\sigma\\sin\\vartheta,\n \\qquad q_* =\\tfrac12\\log(W\\sin^2\\vartheta).$$ With Euclidean strip derivatives, it satisfies $$\\label{a:model-map}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_G(w\\,\\mathrm d\\Phi_*)=0,\\qquad\n -\\Delta q_*=|\\partial\\Phi_*|_G^2,\n \\qquad q_*(0,\\vartheta)=\\log(R^2\\sin\\vartheta).$$ The canonical slice and its tensor and fields extend smoothly to the bifurcation sphere, including the ordinary axis poles.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-c-cut",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a meridional length element in the upper half-plane is $$\\mathrm d\\ell=B(v,\\alpha)\\abs{\\mathrm d(v+i\\alpha)}.$$ Besides the boundary puncture $Z=0$, allow finitely many punctures $a_1,\\ldots,a_N\\in\\mathbb R\\setminus\\{0\\}$. Assume the following.*\n\n1.  *Away from the punctures and the two ends, $B/\\sin\\alpha$ has a positive continuous extension to the closed strip.*\n\n2.  *As $v\\to-\\infty$, this quotient converges uniformly to a positive continuous function $b_-(\\alpha)$. As $v\\to+\\infty$, $$\\frac{B(v,\\alpha)}{c_+e^{2v}\\sin\\alpha}\\longrightarrow1\n     \\quad\\hbox{uniformly},\\qquad c_+>0.$$ At the sides, these statements concern the continuous quotients.*\n\n3.  *Near each $a_j$, put $t=-\\log\\abs{Z-a_j}$ and $\\beta=\\arg(Z-a_j)$. For sufficiently large $t$, the same length element has the form $$B_j(t,\\beta)\\abs{\\mathrm d(-t+i\\beta)},\\qquad\n     c_j\\sin\\beta\\le B_j(t,\\beta)\\le C_j\\sin\\beta,\n     \\qquad 0<c_j\\le C_j<\\infty.$$*\n\n\n*If every crossing has length at least $L>0$, then, for every $k>0$ and $0<L'<L$, there is a holomorphic univalent map on $\\abs{\\xi}>1$, smooth with nonvanishing derivative on $\\abs{\\xi}=1$, such that $$f(\\overline\\xi)=\\overline{f(\\xi)},\\qquad\n f(\\xi)=k\\xi+O(1)\\quad(\\xi\\to\\infty).$$ Its boundary is a smooth Jordan curve enclosing $0$, its upper semicircle maps to a cut avoiding all additional punctures, and, in the coordinates $\\xi=e^{\\sigma+i\\vartheta}$, $$\\label{c:density}\n \\left.\\frac{\\mathrm d\\ell}{\\mathrm d\\vartheta}\\right|_{\\sigma=0}\n \\ge \\frac{L'}2\\sin\\vartheta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\vartheta\\le\\pi.$$ Additional punctures not enclosed by the cut are marked points on the real sides of the parametrized exterior. The constants used to construct $f$ may depend on $k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-c-lower-weight",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#c:cut\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:cut\">11</a>, for every $\\ell<L$ there is a weight $$W(v,\\alpha)=b_0(v,\\alpha)\\sin\\alpha\\le B(v,\\alpha)$$ whose crossing lengths are at least $\\ell$, where $b_0$ is smooth, has even reflection at both sides, satisfies $0<m_0\\le b_0\\le M_0<\\infty$, and is independent of $v$ on each sufficiently far end.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-c-angular",
        "exact_quote": "For every $L'<L$ there are a lower weight $W$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#c:lower-weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:lower-weight\">12</a>, a number $L_1>L'$, and a smooth angular coordinate $\\Theta:\\overline{\\mathcal S}\\to[0,\\pi]$ such that $$\\label{c:calibration}\n \\varphi=\\cos\\Theta,\\qquad\n \\abs{\\mathrm d\\varphi}_{\\mathrm{flat}}\n \\le\\frac{2}{L_1}W\\le\\frac{2}{L_1}B.$$ Here $\\Theta=0,\\pi$ on the respective sides, $\\mathrm d\\Theta$ never vanishes, $\\Theta$ has ordinary odd reflection at both sides, and on each far end it is an increasing angular diffeomorphism independent of $v$. Moreover, $\\Theta$ is joined to $\\alpha$ by a smooth family of angular coordinates with these properties, product on uniform far ends; the estimate in Equation <a href=\"#c:calibration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c:calibration\">[c:calibration]</a> is needed only at the final member.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-c-angular",
          "exact_quote": "For every $L'<L$ there are a lower weight $W$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#c:lower-weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:lower-weight\">12</a>, a number $L_1>L'$, and a smooth angular coordinate $\\Theta:\\overline{\\mathcal S}\\to[0,\\pi]$ such that $$\\label{c:calibration}\n \\varphi=\\cos\\Theta,\\qquad\n \\abs{\\mathrm d\\varphi}_{\\mathrm{flat}}\n \\le\\frac{2}{L_1}W\\le\\frac{2}{L_1}B.$$ Here $\\Theta=0,\\pi$ on the respective sides, $\\mathrm d\\Theta$ never vanishes, $\\Theta$ has ordinary odd reflection at both sides, and on each far end it is an increasing angular diffeomorphism independent of $v$. Moreover, $\\Theta$ is joined to $\\alpha$ by a smooth family of angular coordinates with these properties, product on uniform far ends; the estimate in Equation <a href=\"#c:calibration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c:calibration\">[c:calibration]</a> is needed only at the final member.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $L'<L$ there are a lower weight $W$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#c:lower-weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:lower-weight\">12</a>, a number $L_1>L'$, and a smooth angular coordinate $\\Theta:\\overline{\\mathcal S}\\to[0,\\pi]$ such that $$\\label{c:calibration}\n \\varphi=\\cos\\Theta,\\qquad\n \\abs{\\mathrm d\\varphi}_{\\mathrm{flat}}\n \\le\\frac{2}{L_1}W\\le\\frac{2}{L_1}B.$$ Here $\\Theta=0,\\pi$ on the respective sides, $\\mathrm d\\Theta$ never vanishes, $\\Theta$ has ordinary odd reflection at both sides, and on each far end it is an increasing angular diffeomorphism independent of $v$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-c-analytic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Theta$ be an angular coordinate with the product-end, reflection, and isotopy properties of Lemma <a href=\"#c:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:angular\">13</a>. For every $k>0$ there is a real-symmetric holomorphic function $U$ on $\\abs{\\xi}>1$, smooth up to the circle and with $U(\\infty)=0$, such that $$\\label{c:boundary-equation}\n \\Theta\\bigl(\\log k+\\Re U(e^{i\\vartheta}),\n                   \\vartheta+\\Im U(e^{i\\vartheta})\\bigr)=\\vartheta\n \\qquad(\\vartheta\\in\\mathbb R).$$ The map $f(\\xi)=k\\xi e^{U(\\xi)}$ is univalent on the exterior, and its boundary is a smooth regular Jordan curve enclosing zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-c-analytic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Theta$ be an angular coordinate with the product-end, reflection, and isotopy properties of Lemma <a href=\"#c:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"c:angular\">13</a>.         }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega_r$ be the closure of the component toward infinity obtained by removing finitely many smooth compact invariant obstacles from a rotation exterior as in the axial reduction. The omitted region includes the original inner side, and $\\partial\\Omega_r$ is nonempty. Suppose the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The polarized metric $g_r$ is smooth up to its inner faces, asymptotically flat of order $q_d>1/2$ through at least two derivatives, and has finite ADM energy $E_r$.*\n\n2.  *The potentials are restrictions of potentials on the original rotation exterior. Their constants on its two axis sides are $(-Q,0,-2J)$ and $(Q,0,2J)$, and they have the polar and end regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a> and Equation <a href=\"#a:end-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:end-regularity\">[a:end-regularity]</a>.*\n\n3.  *One has $$\\label{r:scalar-hypothesis}\n     R_{g_r}\\ge 2|\\mathcal D_{X_r}|_{g_r}^2,\\qquad R_{g_r}>0.$$ Each inner face has strictly negative mean curvature for the normal toward infinity. Alternatively, the inner boundary is already a smooth invariant stable minimal boundary.*\n\n4.  *Every meridional path in the retained region joining the two original axis sides has $\\mathfrak h_r$-length at least $L>0$. The same assumption may be verified by a length-decreasing projection from any added collars to a reference meridian with that lower bound.*\n\n*For every $R$ on the open physical branch $R^2>\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, let $M=F_*(R)$ and $b>0$ be the model parameters in Lemma <a href=\"#a:model-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:model-parameters\">9</a>. Then $$\\label{r:mass-bound}\n E_r\\ge F_*(R)+\\frac b2\\log\\frac{L}{2R^2}.$$ If the given inner boundary is a single stable minimal sphere, the strict inequality $R_{g_r}>0$ may be omitted.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega_r$ be the closure of the component toward infinity obtained by removing finitely many smooth compact invariant obstacles from a rotation exterior as in the axial reduction. Their constants on its two axis sides are $(-Q,0,-2J)$ and $(Q,0,2J)$, and they have the polar and end regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a> and Equation <a href=\"#a:end-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:end-regularity\">[a:end-regularity]</a>.*\n\n3.  *One has $$\\label{r:scalar-hypothesis}\n     R_{g_r}\\ge 2|\\mathcal D_{X_r}|_{g_r}^2,\\qquad R_{g_r}>0.$$ Each inner face has strictly negative mean curvature for the normal toward infinity.           "exact_quote": "The potentials are restrictions of potentials on the original rotation exterior.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Their constants on its two axis sides are $(-Q,0,-2J)$ and $(Q,0,2J)$, and they have the polar and end regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a> and Equation <a href=\"#a:end-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:end-regularity\">[a:end-regularity]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "One has $$\\label{r:scalar-hypothesis}\n     R_{g_r}\\ge 2|\\mathcal D_{X_r}|_{g_r}^2,\\qquad R_{g_r}>0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Each inner face has strictly negative mean curvature for the normal toward infinity.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Alternatively, the inner boundary is already a smooth invariant stable minimal boundary.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Every meridional path in the retained region joining the two original axis sides has $\\mathfrak h_r$-length at least $L>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-riemannian-logarithmic-comparison",

          "exact_quote": "For every $R$ on the open physical branch $R^2>\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$,",

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-r-optimized",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#r:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"r:comparison\">15</a>, if $L/2\\ge\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, then $$\\label{r:optimized-bound}\n E_r\\ge F_*\\!\\left(\\sqrt{L/2}\\right).$$ In particular, with $L=A_0/(2\\pi)$ this becomes $$E_r\\ge\\sqrt{\\frac{A_0}{16\\pi}+\\frac{Q^2}{2}\n                  +\\frac{\\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A_0}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-optimized",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#r:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"r:comparison\">15</a>, if $L/2\\ge\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, then $$\\label{r:optimized-bound}\n E_r\\ge F_*\\!\\left(\\sqrt{L/2}\\right).$$ In particular, with $L=A_0/(2\\pi)$ this becomes $$E_r\\ge\\sqrt{\\frac{A_0}{16\\pi}+\\frac{Q^2}{2}\n                  +\\frac{\\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A_0}}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-generalized",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a smooth polarized rotation exterior has one asymptotically flat end, a compact weakly future trapped boundary, and the topology and potential regularity of the axial construction. Assume $g-\\delta=O_2(r^{-1})$, $k=O_1(r^{-3})$, $|s|+|\\mathcal D_X|_g=O(r^{-2})$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\\delta})$ for some $0<\\delta<1$, and finite ADM energy. The potentials have the axis and end regularity in Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a>. On each signed axis tube, $s_1,s_2$ are smooth odd functions of order $y$, and $s_3$ is smooth even of order $y^2$, with smooth longitudinal derivatives. For each row, the functions $|D_i|_g^2,s_i^2$ and the covector $s_iD_i$ extend smoothly to the three-dimensional exterior through the axis. The source expressions below use these extensions at axis points; neither an individual normalized row nor its ordinary norm is required to extend smoothly. Suppose, at every point and for every $w\\in T\\Omega$ with $|w|_g\\le1$, that $$\\label{d:sourced}\n 8\\pi\\bigl(\\mu+J_{\\rm con}(w)\\bigr)\n \\ge |\\mathcal D_X|_g^2+|s|^2+2s\\cdot\\mathcal D_X(w).$$ Let $L>0$ be a lower bound for all crossing lengths in the meridional area metric of $g$. For each admissible subextremal model radius $R$, with $F_*(R)$ and $b=b(R)>0$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#a:model-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:model-parameters\">9</a>, $$\\label{d:sourced-comparison}\n E_g\\ge F_*(R)+\\frac b2\\log\\frac{L}{2R^2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-deformation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,g,k)$ be a smooth polarized one-ended exterior, complete with its compact nonempty boundary included. Assume the boundary is strictly future trapped, $k$ has compact support, $g-\\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every finite $j$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\\delta})$ for some $0<\\delta<1$, and $g$ has finite ADM energy. Let $\\mathfrak m>0$ be a smooth invariant function such that $$\\mathfrak m\\le8\\pi(\\mu-|J_{\\rm con}|_g),\\qquad\n \\inf_C\\mathfrak m>0\\quad\\text{for every compact }C\\subset\\overline\\Omega,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak m\\ge c r^{-3-\\delta}\\quad\\text{on the end}$$ for some $c>0$. There is an invariant reduced exterior $\\Omega_{\\rm red}\\subset\\Omega$, obtained by removing full trapped regions and retaining the component toward infinity, with smooth nonempty compact boundary, for which the following holds. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, and all sufficiently large integers $n$ along a sequence, there are smooth invariant functions $f_n,t_n$ and positive smooth profiles $l_n(t)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\label{d:deformed-metrics}\n \\bar g_n=g+l_n(t_n)^2\\mathrm df_n^2,\\qquad\n \\hat g_n=e^{4t_n}\\bar g_n,\\qquad\n w_n=\\frac{l_n(t_n)\\nabla f_n}{\\sqrt{1+l_n(t_n)^2|\\mathrm df_n|_g^2}},\n \\qquad t_n\\ge-\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\frac12e^{4t_n}R_{\\hat g_n}\n \\ge8\\pi\\bigl(\\mu+J_{\\rm con}(w_n)\\bigr)-\\frac{\\mathfrak m}{2}.\n \\label{d:deformation-curvature}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The inner boundary has strictly negative mean curvature in $\\hat g_n$ for its normal toward infinity. The metric $\\hat g_n$ is complete with boundary, is asymptotically flat of order one with at least two controlled derivatives, and has finite energy satisfying $$\\label{d:deformation-energy}\n E_{\\hat g_n}\\le E_g+o_n(1).$$ Here the reduced domain and its collars are fixed before $\\varepsilon$ and $n$. For each fixed $n$ the functions are obtained by exhaustion of finite outer truncations; the error in Equation <a href=\"#d:deformation-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"d:deformation-energy\">[d:deformation-energy]</a> tends to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ after that exhaustion, with the prepared data and $\\varepsilon$ fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-d-deformation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,g,k)$ be a smooth polarized one-ended exterior, complete with its compact nonempty boundary included. Assume the boundary is strictly future trapped, $k$ has compact support, $g-\\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every finite $j$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\\delta})$ for some $0<\\delta<1$, and $g$ has finite ADM energy. Let $\\mathfrak m>0$ be a smooth invariant function such that $$\\mathfrak m\\le8\\pi(\\mu-|J_{\\rm con}|_g),\\qquad\n \\inf_C\\mathfrak m>0\\quad\\text{for every compact }C\\subset\\overline\\Omega,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak m\\ge c r^{-3-\\delta}\\quad\\text{on the end}$$ for some $c>0$. There is an invariant reduced exterior $\\Omega_{\\rm red}\\subset\\Omega$, obtained by removing full trapped regions and retaining the component toward infinity, with smooth nonempty compact boundary, for which the following holds. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, and all sufficiently large integers $n$ along a sequence, there are smooth invariant functions $f_n,t_n$ and positive smooth profiles $l_n(t)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\label{d:deformed-metrics}\n \\bar g_n=g+l_n(t_n)^2\\mathrm df_n^2,\\qquad\n \\hat g_n=e^{4t_n}\\bar g_n,\\qquad\n w_n=\\frac{l_n(t_n)\\nabla f_n}{\\sqrt{1+l_n(t_n)^2|\\mathrm df_n|_g^2}},\n \\qquad t_n\\ge-\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\frac12e^{4t_n}R_{\\hat g_n}\n \\ge8\\pi\\bigl(\\mu+J_{\\rm con}(w_n)\\bigr)-\\frac{\\mathfrak m}{2}.\n \\label{d:deformation-curvature}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The inner boundary has strictly negative mean curvature in $\\hat g_n$ for its normal toward infinity. The metric $\\hat g_n$ is complete with boundary, is asymptotically flat of order one with at least two controlled derivatives, and has finite energy satisfying $$\\label{d:deformation-energy}\n E_{\\hat g_n}\\le E_g+o_n(1).$$ Here the reduced domain and its collars are fixed before $\\varepsilon$ and $n$. For each fixed $n$ the functions are obtained by exhaustion of finite outer truncations; the error in Equation <a href=\"#d:deformation-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"d:deformation-energy\">[d:deformation-energy]</a> tends to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ after that exhaustion, with the prepared data and $\\varepsilon$ fixed.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-weak-support",
        "exact_quote": "Work in a connected compact barrier truncation as above. Let $D$ be a nonempty compact closed set, disjoint from the designated outer faces and containing a collar of any required inner face. Suppose that every smooth exterior support at an interior frontier point of $D$ has expansion at most $c$ for a smooth tensor $Q$. An exterior support means a smooth regular level through the point that contains $D$ locally on its inner side. Then, for every $c'>c$ for which the outer barriers remain strict, $D$ is contained in a smooth bounding region with outward expansion at most $c'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-d-weak-support",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-identities",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\bar g=g+l^2\\mathrm df^2$ and $\\hat g=e^{4t}\\bar g$, and for the actual trace $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{A_\\chi}S_1$, one has $$\\label{d:curvature}\n \\frac12e^{4t}R_{\\hat g}\n =8\\pi\\bigl(\\mu+J_0(w)\\bigr)+\\mathcal T+w(F)-F\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}K\n       -u^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}V,$$ where $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{d:quadratic}\n \\mathcal T={}&\\frac12|T^{\\rm tf}|^2\n +(M+pa\\nabla_\\perp t)\\cdot(M+(p+2)a\\nabla_\\perp t)\n +4(p+1)|\\mathrm dt|_{A_d}^2\\notag\\\\\n &+(\\chi-\\chi^2/4)q^2+2(p+1)\\chi qa\\,\\partial_et\n       -\\frac\\chi2Fq+\\frac34F^2.\n\\end{aligned}$$ It has the square completion $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{d:squares}\n \\mathcal T={}&\\frac12|T^{\\rm tf}|^2\n  +|M+(p+1)a\\nabla_\\perp t|^2\n  +[4(p+1)-v]|\\nabla_\\perp t|^2\\notag\\\\\n &+4(p+1)d\\left(\\partial_et+\\frac{\\chi aq}{4d}\\right)^2\n  +\\frac34\\left(F-\\frac{\\chi q}{3}\\right)^2\n  +\\frac{4d(9-d)}{3(4+pv)^2}q^2.\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $$\\label{d:coercivity}\n |T|^2+|M|^2+dq^2+F^2+|\\mathrm dt|_{A_d}^2\n       \\le\\Pi_n\\mathcal T.$$ Here and below $\\Pi_n$ denotes a positive polynomial bound in $n$; its coefficients may depend on the prepared data, fixed collars and cutoff profiles, but not on the truncation or the solution. Different occurrences may denote different such bounds. If $\\mathrm dt=0$, then, with $Q_f=|T^{\\rm tf}|^2+|M|^2+\\chi q^2+F^2$, $$\\label{d:floor-norm}\n \\tfrac12Q_f\\le\\mathcal T\\le Q_f.$$ At any boundary face on which $f$ is constant, $$\\label{d:boundary-identity}\n \\frac{V_\\nu}{u}\n =d(H+4\\partial_\\nu t)-H+w_\\nu(F-P_B),\\qquad\n H_{\\hat g}=e^{-2t}\\sqrt d\\,(H+4\\partial_\\nu t).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-d-collars",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $n$ and sufficiently large outer truncations, solutions in the first two homotopy stages satisfy, with constants $c,C'>0$ independent of $n,R$ and the stage parameter, $$\\label{d:collar-slopes}\n \\frac c{\\tau_0}\\le\\partial_\\nu f\\le\\frac{C'}{\\tau_0}\\quad(B_b),\\qquad\n \\frac c{\\tau_0}\\le-\\partial_\\nu f\\le\\frac{C'}{\\tau_0}\\quad(B_w).$$ They also satisfy $b_-\\le h\\le b_+$ on any fixed compact core needed in Equation <a href=\"#d:offset-margin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"d:offset-margin\">[d:offset-margin]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-weighted-trace",
        "exact_quote": "In the first two homotopy stages, for every nonnegative smooth $\\varphi$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_B L\\varphi\n &\\le \\Pi_n\\int_{\\mathrm{coll}}u\n \\bigl[(1+\\sqrt{\\mathcal T})\\varphi+\n                         |\\mathrm d\\varphi|_{A_\\chi}\\bigr],\n \\label{e:weighted-trace-eq}\\\\\n \\int_B \\varphi\n &\\le \\Pi_n\\int_{\\mathrm{coll}}\\sqrt D\n \\bigl[(1+\\sqrt{\\mathcal T})\\varphi+\n                         |\\mathrm d\\varphi|_{A_\\chi}\\bigr].\n \\label{e:unweighted-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The integrands on the right include fixed collar cutoffs. In the first stage, moreover, $$\\label{e:small-boundary-flux}\n |(V_\\lambda)_\\nu|\\le Cud\n                 \\le C\\frac{\\tau_0}{\\ell}L.$$ None of these estimates requires an upper bound for $Z$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-gradient",
        "exact_quote": "Consider three-dimensional coordinate patches with a fixed positive lower bound for their size, uniform ellipticity and smooth bounds for their metrics, and, when present, uniform smooth geometry of one boundary face. Let $z$ be a smooth solution, including up to that face, of $$\\label{g:axial-equation}\n A_\\chi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}z=G_0,\\qquad\n A_\\chi=I-(1-\\chi)e\\otimes e,\\qquad\n e=\\frac{\\nabla z}{|\\nabla z|},\\qquad\n 0<\\chi_0\\leq\\chi\\leq1.$$ At a zero of $\\nabla z$ any measurable unit axis for which the equation holds is allowed. Assume $|\\nabla z|\\leq L_0$ and $|G_0|\\leq Q_0$; on a boundary face assume that $z$ is constant. Only the displayed measurable bounds on $\\chi$ and $G_0$ are used. There are $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$, $C<\\infty$, and a uniform smaller patch size, depending on $\\chi_0$ and the geometric bounds, such that $$\\label{g:gradient-estimates}\n \\|D_xz\\|_{C^{0,\\alpha}}\\leq C(L_0+Q_0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_r}|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}z|^2\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n       \\leq C(L_0+Q_0)^2r^{1+2\\alpha}.$$ The first norm is on the smaller patch. The second estimate holds for all sufficiently small balls centered in it, with intersection with the domain understood at a boundary. The same estimate holds for $D_x^2z$ and coordinate volume. These are a priori estimates for the stated smooth solutions, with constants independent of their higher derivatives.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix the prepared data, the selected reduced domain and collars, and all deformation parameters, including $n$ and $\\varepsilon$.         "anchor": "stmt-j-adjoint",
        "exact_quote": "There are smooth invariant fields $N,Y$ on the polarized exterior, with $Y$ meridional, such that $$\\label{j:causal}\n N\\geq |Y|_g,\\qquad N>0\\ \\text{in }\\operatorname{int}\\Omega,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{sym}\\nabla Y=-Nk.$$ For every $q'\\in(1/2,1)$, their asymptotics are $$\\label{j:asymptotic}\n N=1+O_2(r^{-q'}),\\qquad Y=O_2(r^{-2}).$$ At the inner boundary, with $\\nu$ directed into the exterior, $$\\label{j:boundary}\n Y=N\\nu,\\qquad \\partial_\\nu N+k(Y,\\nu)=\\kappa.$$ Furthermore, either $N|_S>0$ everywhere or $N|_S\\equiv0$.*\n\n*On regular orbits in the interior, define $$\\label{j:stationary-metric}\n H_3=-N^2\\mathrm dt_0^2+\n       h_{ij}(\\mathrm dx^i+Y^i\\mathrm dt_0)(\\mathrm dx^j+Y^j\\mathrm dt_0),\n \\qquad \\gamma=X^2H_3.$$ Then $(\\gamma,\\Phi)$, with $\\partial_{t_0}\\Phi=0$, solves the full Einstein–wave-map system of Equation <a href=\"#a:wave\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:wave\">[a:wave]</a>. For the future $H_3$-unit normal $n$ to $t_0=0$, the induced horizontal tensor is $k|_h$, and the map velocities are $$\\mathrm d\\log X(n)=k(e,e),\\qquad \\mathcal D_X(n)=s.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "The infimum defining $a(\\widetilde g)$ is attained by an invariant filled inner set.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-end",
        "exact_quote": "For the adjoint fields above, there are constants $N_\\infty,Y_\\infty$ such that, for every $q'\\in(1/2,1)$, $$(N,Y)=(N_\\infty,Y_\\infty)+O_2(r^{-q'}).$$ The multiplier identity gives $N_\\infty=1$, and positive ADM energy gives $Y_\\infty=0$. In fact $Y=O_2(r^{-2})$.*\n\n*More generally, the elimination of $Y_\\infty$ applies to any pair with these constant asymptotics that satisfies $\\operatorname{sym}\\nabla Y=-Nk$, provided $k=O_1(r^{-3})$, $N$ is bounded, the scalar linearization $L(g-\\delta)=O(r^{-4})$, and the ADM energy is positive.",
```
