<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Enclosing cut** ([source](#stmt-m-cut)): An enclosing cut $\Gamma$ is the full compact intrinsic boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold $D\subset\Omega$, closed as a subset of $\Omega$, whose interior lies in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$ and which contains the entire sufficiently distant end. The boundary is smooth, embedded and two-sided. All its components are counted, including any portion coinciding with $S$. No symmetry or trapping condition is imposed on $\Gamma$.
- **Constraint densities** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Writing $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK$, define the constraint densities by $$\label{m:densities} 16\pi\mu=R_g+\tau^2-\abs{K}_g^2,\qquad 8\pi(J_{\mathrm{con}})_i=\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij}).$$
- **Rest-frame mass and total charge magnitude** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Assume $P_{\mathrm{ADM}}=0$ and $E_{\mathrm{ADM}}>0$, and set $$\label{m:mass-charge} m=E_{\mathrm{ADM}}=\sqrt{E_{\mathrm{ADM}}^2-\abs{P_{\mathrm{ADM}}}^2}, \qquad Q=\sqrt{Q_e^2+Q_b^2}.$$
- **Electromagnetically corrected total angular momentum** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For an invariant enclosing surface $\Sigma$, with normal toward infinity, define the total angular momentum $$\label{m:total-angular} J_{\mathrm{tot}}(\Sigma)=\frac1{8\pi}\int_\Sigma(K-\tau g)(\nu,\eta)\,\mathrm dA_g +\frac1{4\pi}\int_\Sigma\alpha(\eta)g(E',\nu)\,\mathrm dA_g.$$
- **Polarized initial data** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Define $$\label{a:polarized-data} g_0=h+X^2\mathrm d\phi^2, \qquad k_0=K\|_{H\times H}+K(e,e)X^2\mathrm d\phi^2.$$
- **Comparison mass function and charge-angular combination** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Set $$C=Q^4+4J^2,\qquad F_*(R)=\frac12\sqrt{R^2+2Q^2+C/R^2}.$$
- **Meridional area metric** ([source](#r:section)): The comparison uses the length metric $\mathfrak h_r=X_r^2h_r$ on the meridian of a polarized metric $g_r=h_r+X_r^2\mathrm d\phi^2$.
- **Minimum enclosing area** ([source](#pm-h-58)): For a polarized metric $\widetilde g$ near $g$, let $a(\widetilde g)$ be the minimum enclosing area.
- **Quotient Killing norm** ([source](#pm-h-66)): Define $$\label{k:norm} \mu_*=N^2-\|Y\|_h^2,\qquad \alpha_Y=h(Y,\cdot).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-penrose-squared-mass | m^2 | ≥ | $\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.$ | The physical area branch is assumed; the numerical bound includes its closed endpoint. |
| q-model-mass | M | = | $F_*(R)$ | Fixed charge and total angular momentum; model radius on the open physical branch. |
| q-model-radial-scale | b | = | $\frac{R^4-C}{4MR^2}$ | The mass and rotation parameters are those defined in the model-parameter lemma. |
| q-model-mass-derivative | F_*'(R) | = | $\frac bR$ | Differentiation varies the model radius with conserved quantities fixed. |
| q-model-angular-sign | J | = | $-aM$ | The prescribed increasing azimuth and positive future-normal tensor convention. |
| q-cut-boundary-density | \left.\frac{\mathrm d\ell}{\mathrm d\vartheta}\right\|_{\sigma=0} | ≥ | $\frac{L'}2\sin\vartheta$ | Boundary density on the upper semicircle of the constructed cut; capacity is prescribed independently. |
| q-riemannian-energy-comparison | E_r | ≥ | $F_*(R)+\frac b2\log\frac{L}{2R^2}$ | The crossing lower bound is fixed while the subextremal model radius varies. |
| q-crossing-area-conversion | L | = | $\frac{A_0}{2\pi}$ | The lower bound follows by smoothing and rotating simple crossing arcs. |
| q-optimized-comparison-energy | E_r | ≥ | $F_*\!\left(\sqrt{L/2}\right)$ | The endpoint is obtained by continuity through subextremal models. |
| q-deformation-conformal-floor | t_n | ≥ | $-\varepsilon$ | The reduced domain is fixed before the conformal floor and the deformation parameter. |
| q-fixed-parameter-hessian-mass | \int_{B_r}\|\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}z\|^2\,\mathrm dV_g | ≤ | $C(L_0+Q_0)^2r^{1+2\alpha}$ | Constants depend on the lower ellipticity bound and geometric bounds, independently of higher derivatives of the smooth solution. |
| q-deformed-energy-flux-identity | E_{\hat g} | = | $E_g-\frac{\mathfrak F_n}{8\pi}$ | The normal convention and bulk-plus-boundary flux are those of the end-asymptotics lemma. |
| q-vanishing-negative-flux | \mathfrak F_n | ≥ | $-o_n(1)$ | Exhaust the outer truncations at fixed deformation parameter before passing to its limit. |
| q-equality-surface-gravity | \kappa | = | $\frac{b}{R_0^2}$ | The area radius and model parameters are fixed by the original conserved quantities and boundary area. |
| q-static-boundary-area-density | X\sqrt{P_+} | = | $R_0^2\sin\vartheta$ | The inner edge of the global static isothermal strip. |

          "exact_quote": "For every $R>0$ with $R^2>\\sqrt C$, define $$M=F_*(R),\\qquad a=-J/M,\\qquad\n b=\\sqrt{M^2-a^2-Q^2}.$$ Then $$\\label{a:parameter-identities}\n b=\\frac{R^4-C}{4MR^2}>0,\\qquad\n (M+b)^2+a^2=R^2,\\qquad F_*'(R)=\\frac bR.$$ The function $b(R)$ is strictly increasing and maps this branch onto $(0,\\infty)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-a-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Phi_*$ be the map of Proposition <a href=\"#a:quotient-proposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:quotient-proposition\">7</a> for Equation <a href=\"#a:kn-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:kn-metric\">[a:kn-metric]</a>, with axis values Equation <a href=\"#a:axis-values\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-values\">[a:axis-values]</a>. Its ADM energy is $M$, its angular momentum in the present convention is $J=-aM$, its charges are $Q,0$, and its horizon area is $4\\pi R^2$. It is independent of Killing time. Define $$w=\\sinh\\sigma\\sin\\vartheta,\n \\qquad q_* =\\tfrac12\\log(W\\sin^2\\vartheta).$$ With Euclidean strip derivatives, it satisfies $$\\label{a:model-map}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_G(w\\,\\mathrm d\\Phi_*)=0,\\qquad\n -\\Delta q_*=|\\partial\\Phi_*|_G^2,\n \\qquad q_*(0,\\vartheta)=\\log(R^2\\sin\\vartheta).$$ The canonical slice and its tensor and fields extend smoothly to the bifurcation sphere, including the ordinary axis poles. Its potentials have the regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a>; their renormalized components and $u_*-\\log\\sin\\vartheta$ have bounded derivatives on compact closed strip ranges.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-c-cut",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a meridional length element in the upper half-plane is $$\\mathrm d\\ell=B(v,\\alpha)\\abs{\\mathrm d(v+i\\alpha)}.$$ Besides the boundary puncture $Z=0$, allow finitely many punctures $a_1,\\ldots,a_N\\in\\mathbb R\\setminus\\{0\\}$. Assume the following.*\n\n1.  *Away from the punctures and the two ends, $B/\\sin\\alpha$ has a positive continuous extension to the closed strip.*\n\n2.  *As $v\\to-\\infty$, this quotient converges uniformly to a positive continuous function $b_-(\\alpha)$. As $v\\to+\\infty$, $$\\frac{B(v,\\alpha)}{c_+e^{2v}\\sin\\alpha}\\longrightarrow1\n     \\quad\\hbox{uniformly},\\qquad c_+>0.$$ At the sides, these statements concern the continuous quotients.*\n\n3.  *Near each $a_j$, put $t=-\\log\\abs{Z-a_j}$ and $\\beta=\\arg(Z-a_j)$. For sufficiently large $t$, the same length element has the form $$B_j(t,\\beta)\\abs{\\mathrm d(-t+i\\beta)},\\qquad\n     c_j\\sin\\beta\\le B_j(t,\\beta)\\le C_j\\sin\\beta,\n     \\qquad 0<c_j\\le C_j<\\infty.$$*\n\n\n*If every crossing has length at least $L>0$, then, for every $k>0$ and $0<L'<L$, there is a holomorphic univalent map on $\\abs{\\xi}>1$, smooth with nonvanishing derivative on $\\abs{\\xi}=1$, such that $$f(\\overline\\xi)=\\overline{f(\\xi)},\\qquad\n f(\\xi)=k\\xi+O(1)\\quad(\\xi\\to\\infty).$$ Its boundary is a smooth Jordan curve enclosing $0$, its upper semicircle maps to a cut avoiding all additional punctures, and, in the coordinates $\\xi=e^{\\sigma+i\\vartheta}$, $$\\label{c:density}\n \\left.\\frac{\\mathrm d\\ell}{\\mathrm d\\vartheta}\\right|_{\\sigma=0}\n \\ge \\frac{L'}2\\sin\\vartheta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\vartheta\\le\\pi.$$ Additional punctures not enclosed by the cut are marked points on the real sides of the parametrized exterior. The constants used to construct $f$ may depend on $k$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-r-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega_r$ be the closure of the component toward infinity obtained by removing finitely many smooth compact invariant obstacles from a rotation exterior as in the axial reduction. The omitted region includes the original inner side, and $\\partial\\Omega_r$ is nonempty. Suppose the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The polarized metric $g_r$ is smooth up to its inner faces, asymptotically flat of order $q_d>1/2$ through at least two derivatives, and has finite ADM energy $E_r$.*\n\n2.  *The potentials are restrictions of potentials on the original rotation exterior. Their constants on its two axis sides are $(-Q,0,-2J)$ and $(Q,0,2J)$, and they have the polar and end regularity of Lemma <a href=\"#a:axis-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:axis-regularity\">5</a> and Equation <a href=\"#a:end-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:end-regularity\">[a:end-regularity]</a>.*\n\n3.  *One has $$\\label{r:scalar-hypothesis}\n     R_{g_r}\\ge 2|\\mathcal D_{X_r}|_{g_r}^2,\\qquad R_{g_r}>0.$$ Each inner face has strictly negative mean curvature for the normal toward infinity. Alternatively, the inner boundary is already a smooth invariant stable minimal boundary.*\n\n4.  *Every meridional path in the retained region joining the two original axis sides has $\\mathfrak h_r$-length at least $L>0$. The same assumption may be verified by a length-decreasing projection from any added collars to a reference meridian with that lower bound.*\n\n*For every $R$ on the open physical branch $R^2>\\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$, let $M=F_*(R)$ and $b>0$ be the model parameters in Lemma <a href=\"#a:model-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:model-parameters\">9</a>. Then $$\\label{r:mass-bound}\n E_r\\ge F_*(R)+\\frac b2\\log\\frac{L}{2R^2}.$$ If the given inner boundary is a single stable minimal sphere, the strict inequality $R_{g_r}>0$ may be omitted.",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-d-deformation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,g,k)$ be a smooth polarized one-ended exterior, complete with its compact nonempty boundary included. Assume the boundary is strictly future trapped, $k$ has compact support, $g-\\delta=O_j(r^{-1})$ for every finite $j$, $R_g=O(r^{-3-\\delta})$ for some $0<\\delta<1$, and $g$ has finite ADM energy. Let $\\mathfrak m>0$ be a smooth invariant function such that $$\\mathfrak m\\le8\\pi(\\mu-|J_{\\rm con}|_g),\\qquad\n \\inf_C\\mathfrak m>0\\quad\\text{for every compact }C\\subset\\overline\\Omega,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak m\\ge c r^{-3-\\delta}\\quad\\text{on the end}$$ for some $c>0$. There is an invariant reduced exterior $\\Omega_{\\rm red}\\subset\\Omega$, obtained by removing full trapped regions and retaining the component toward infinity, with smooth nonempty compact boundary, for which the following holds. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, and all sufficiently large integers $n$ along a sequence, there are smooth invariant functions $f_n,t_n$ and positive smooth profiles $l_n(t)$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\label{d:deformed-metrics}\n \\bar g_n=g+l_n(t_n)^2\\mathrm df_n^2,\\qquad\n \\hat g_n=e^{4t_n}\\bar g_n,\\qquad\n w_n=\\frac{l_n(t_n)\\nabla f_n}{\\sqrt{1+l_n(t_n)^2|\\mathrm df_n|_g^2}},\n \\qquad t_n\\ge-\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\frac12e^{4t_n}R_{\\hat g_n}\n \\ge8\\pi\\bigl(\\mu+J_{\\rm con}(w_n)\\bigr)-\\frac{\\mathfrak m}{2}.\n \\label{d:deformation-curvature}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The inner boundary has strictly negative mean curvature in $\\hat g_n$ for its normal toward infinity. The metric $\\hat g_n$ is complete with boundary, is asymptotically flat of order one with at least two controlled derivatives, and has finite energy satisfying $$\\label{d:deformation-energy}\n E_{\\hat g_n}\\le E_g+o_n(1).$$ Here the reduced domain and its collars are fixed before $\\varepsilon$ and $n$. For each fixed $n$ the functions are obtained by exhaustion of finite outer truncations; the error in Equation <a href=\"#d:deformation-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"d:deformation-energy\">[d:deformation-energy]</a> tends to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ after that exhaustion, with the prepared data and $\\varepsilon$ fixed.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-gradient",
          "exact_quote": "Consider three-dimensional coordinate patches with a fixed positive lower bound for their size, uniform ellipticity and smooth bounds for their metrics, and, when present, uniform smooth geometry of one boundary face. Let $z$ be a smooth solution, including up to that face, of $$\\label{g:axial-equation}\n A_\\chi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}z=G_0,\\qquad\n A_\\chi=I-(1-\\chi)e\\otimes e,\\qquad\n e=\\frac{\\nabla z}{|\\nabla z|},\\qquad\n 0<\\chi_0\\leq\\chi\\leq1.$$ At a zero of $\\nabla z$ any measurable unit axis for which the equation holds is allowed. Assume $|\\nabla z|\\leq L_0$ and $|G_0|\\leq Q_0$; on a boundary face assume that $z$ is constant. Only the displayed measurable bounds on $\\chi$ and $G_0$ are used. There are $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$, $C<\\infty$, and a uniform smaller patch size, depending on $\\chi_0$ and the geometric bounds, such that $$\\label{g:gradient-estimates}\n \\|D_xz\\|_{C^{0,\\alpha}}\\leq C(L_0+Q_0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_r}|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}z|^2\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n       \\leq C(L_0+Q_0)^2r^{1+2\\alpha}.$$ The first norm is on the smaller patch. The second estimate holds for all sufficiently small balls centered in it, with intersection with the domain understood at a boundary. The same estimate holds for $D_x^2z$ and coordinate volume. These are a priori estimates for the stated smooth solutions, with constants independent of their higher derivatives.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.4"
        }

            "exact_quote": "There are global static isothermal coordinates $(\\sigma,\\vartheta)\\in[0,\\infty)\\times[0,\\pi]$, with the inner boundary $\\sigma=0$ and the two oriented axes $\\vartheta=0,\\pi$, in which $$\\label{k:rho}\n h_+=P_+(\\mathrm d\\sigma^2+\\mathrm d\\vartheta^2),\\qquad\n \\rho=b\\sinh\\sigma\\sin\\vartheta .$$ The boundary area density is $$\\label{k:area-density}\n X\\sqrt{P_+}=\\frac b\\kappa\\sin\\vartheta\n                 =R_0^2\\sin\\vartheta\\qquad(\\sigma=0).$$ In particular, the scale $b$ is determined by the original area and the adjoint boundary condition.",

          }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-orbit-topology** [claim](#stmt-a-topology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-topology): Cap the boundary spheres equivariantly, exclude nontrivial orbifold fundamental group and patch angular gauges relative to the end. -> A half-annulus quotient without exceptional orbits and a global compatible angular gauge.
- **step-magnetic-primitive-flux** [claim](#stmt-a-angular-momentum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-angular-momentum): Use zero magnetic period to construct and glue decaying primitives, average over rotations and apply the contracted momentum constraint. -> A smooth decaying magnetic primitive and conserved corrected angular momentum.
- **step-axis-end-potentials** [claim](#stmt-a-axis-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-axis-regularity): Integrate the potential equations using invariant polar parity and repeat the estimates on rescaled end annuli. -> Axis parity, corrected central vanishing and uniform differentiated end estimates.
- **step-polarization** [claim](#stmt-a-polarization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-polarization): Use polar Schur complements for smoothness, reflection for the energy flux and connection formulas for exact source identities. -> Polarized data preserving invariant areas, expansions and energy with a smooth sourced constraint identity.
- **step-spacetime-quotient** [claim](#stmt-a-quotient-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-quotient-proposition): Compute Maxwell contractions and Lorentzian curvature blocks, then reverse the reduced equations to reconstruct the local spacetime. -> The Einstein–wave-map quotient and local reconstruction of all original slice components.
- **step-target-geometry** [claim](#stmt-a-target-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-target-geometry): Exclude finite-length escaping paths, compute the target curvature form and apply geodesic comparison. -> Completeness and nonpositive curvature with unique geodesics and convex squared distance.
- **step-model-parameters** [claim](#stmt-a-model-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-model-parameters): Substitute the model mass formula, clear denominators and differentiate the parameter relations. -> The subextremal identities, mass derivative and strict monotonicity of radial scale.
- **step-model-map** [claim](#stmt-a-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-model): Evaluate the explicit Kerr–Newman metric and potentials, verify flux normalizations and apply the quotient equations. -> The stationary weighted harmonic map, logarithmic density equation and smooth horizon boundary data.
- **step-smooth-lower-weight** [claim](#stmt-c-lower-weight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-lower-weight): Remove puncture tails with controlled crossing losses, saturate the positive end and smooth the quotient weight from below. -> A smooth positive bounded lower quotient weight with reflection parity and product ends.
- **step-angular-calibration** [claim](#stmt-c-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-angular): Smooth a truncated distance calibration, retain essential regular level bands, extend them to a foliation and compress bad labels. -> An angular submersion with the calibrated gradient bound, product ends and an admissible isotopy.
- **step-analytic-boundary-problem** [claim](#stmt-c-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-analytic): Continue along the angular isotopy using Schwarz phase multipliers, uniform range and boundary derivative bounds, then apply the argument principle. -> A normalized exterior analytic solution and a univalent map with smooth Jordan boundary.
- **step-conformal-cut** [claim](#stmt-c-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-analytic-2): Compose the calibration with the analytic boundary solution and use strict slack to translate away any puncture endpoints. -> A smooth cut avoiding additional punctures and satisfying the pointwise length-density lower bound.
- **step-riemannian-comparison** [claim](#stmt-r-comparison) / [proof](#r:weak-comparison): Construct stable invariant hulls, attach length-decreasing cylinders, uniformize, apply the conformal cut and integrate the target-convexity flux with every boundary term controlled. -> The logarithmic ADM energy comparison for each admissible model radius.
- **step-optimize-comparison** [claim](#stmt-r-optimized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-optimized): Differentiate the model comparison expression and use monotonicity of radial scale; pass numerically to the closed endpoint. -> The optimized model mass at the crossing-area radius.
- **step-support-enclosure** [claim](#stmt-d-weak-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-weak-support): Transfer exterior support inequalities to small parallel sets with curvature-independent error, then use strict trapped-surface barriers to obtain a smooth enclosure. -> A smooth enclosing region at a slightly larger permissible expansion threshold.
- **step-curvature-square-identities** [claim](#stmt-d-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-identities): Compare stationary Lorentzian and static warped-product scalar expressions, insert the constraints and complete the transverse and longitudinal squares. -> Exact curvature, coercivity and boundary mean-curvature identities.
- **step-floor-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-d-floor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-floor): Exclude boundary floor contact by the normal derivative sign and interior contact by comparing graph curvature with the penalized divergence source. -> No floor contact for sufficiently large finite truncations throughout the homotopy.
- **step-height-separation** [claim](#stmt-d-height-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-height-separation): Pass to relaxed height limits, convert lower tests to exterior supports of closed sublevels and contradict right continuity of full trapped regions. -> Uniform strict height gaps away from the corresponding full trapped region.
- **step-collar-slopes** [claim](#stmt-d-collars) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-collars): Compare with collar height barriers whose logarithmic slopes control the small longitudinal ellipticity errors. -> Signed normal slope bounds and compact-core height confinement without a prior ceiling.
- **step-weighted-traces** [claim](#stmt-e-weighted-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-weighted-trace): Apply the divergence theorem to signed weighted graph-direction fields with collar cutoffs and use coercivity. -> Weighted boundary traces and the small first-stage inner flux.
- **step-homotopy-ceiling** [claim](#stmt-e-ceiling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-ceiling): Obtain a high-source integral bound, establish a graph Sobolev inequality with controlled boundary traces and iterate exponential tests to a supremum bound. -> A bounded variable range at fixed deformation parameters, uniform over homotopy stages and large truncations.
- **step-local-gradient-estimate** [claim](#stmt-g-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-gradient): Combine reflected Cordes absorption, a gradient-deficit flux identity and a drop-or-affine improvement iteration, then estimate Hessian mass after subtracting affine approximants. -> Uniform local gradient Hölder estimates and Hessian-square growth bounds.
- **step-coupled-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-g-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-regularity): Control the Hessian-square source as a measure, derive conformal-variable oscillation decay by exponential transformation and Green corrections, then absorb quadratic gradients and bootstrap. -> Uniform local classical bounds through every prescribed finite order at fixed parameters.
- **step-scalar-trace-solve** [claim](#stmt-e-trace-solve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-trace-solve): Interpolate the trace equation with a linear Dirichlet problem, use logarithmic barriers and a two-point gradient comparison, then apply continuation and uniqueness. -> A unique continuous regularizing scalar solve without a floor assumption.
- **step-physical-existence** [claim](#stmt-e-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-existence): Combine the scalar solve with a frozen linear divergence solve to define a compact homotopy map, preserve degree inside the floor constraint and exhaust the truncations. -> A smooth invariant physical solution on the reduced exterior.
- **step-end-asymptotics-flux** [claim](#stmt-e-asymptotics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-asymptotics): Use exponential barriers for the graph equation, transform the remaining end equation to a Poisson equation and identify the conformal ADM flux. -> Asymptotic flatness, graph decay and the exact energy-flux identity.
- **step-negative-flux-absorption** [claim](#stmt-e-flux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-flux): Absorb the small inner flux with weighted traces and the penalty with the positive graph-height source, leaving integrable exponentially small remainders. -> Vanishing negative flux and the asymptotic upper bound on deformation energy.
- **step-complete-neutral-deformation** [claim](#stmt-d-deformation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-flux-2): Insert the physical source into the curvature identity, control offset errors by the prescribed margin and use the boundary identity and end-flux estimate. -> The neutral deformation theorem with prescribed scalar loss, negative inner mean curvature, completeness and energy control.
- **step-sourced-comparison** [claim](#stmt-d-generalized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-deformation): Strictify conformally, prepare the end, apply the neutral deformation, complete each source row and use monotonicity of the meridional area metric before removing the preparation. -> The logarithmic comparison for the enlarged sourced class.
- **step-numerical-original-bound** [claim](#stmt-d-numerical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-numerical): Apply the polarized source identities, convert minimum enclosing area to crossing length and optimize the model radius, using continuity at the closed endpoint. -> The numerical Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality and its strict-branch sourced extension.
- **step-area-envelope** [claim](#stmt-j-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-area): Use obstacle perimeter compactness, choose an invariant maximal-volume minimizer and compare perturbed minima using convergence of area and tangent-plane measures. -> A compact minimizing family and its one-sided area envelope derivative.
- **step-positive-multipliers** [claim](#stmt-j-multipliers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-multipliers): Use the strict feasible direction and numerical bound to separate the variation image from the positive cone, then represent the functional by nonnegative measures. -> A normalized positive multiplier identity including boundary and possible infinity terms.
- **step-cut-measure-concentration** [claim](#stmt-j-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-concentration): Test with exact compact spacelike slice changes, identify zero tangential tensor trace on minimizing frontiers and invoke MOTS regularity and outermostness. -> Concentration of the minimizing-family probability measure on the original boundary.
- **step-moment-regularity** [claim](#stmt-j-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-regularity): Take moment distributions, use the coupled elliptic lapse-shift system, prolong the metric and shift equations and test independent boundary jets. -> Smooth lapse and shift through the axis and up to the boundary, without boundary-supported moment mass.
- **step-end-normalization** [claim](#stmt-j-end) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-end): Integrate the first-jet system along rays, normalize the lapse with an energy variation and eliminate spatial translation by an affine ADM flux identity. -> Unit limiting lapse, zero limiting translation and improved shift decay.
- **step-boundary-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-j-dichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-j-dichotomy): Use compact normal displacement variations to derive the adjoint MOTS stability equation and apply the strong minimum principle with nonnegative mean curvature. -> The positive-or-identically-zero boundary lapse dichotomy.
- **step-stationary-adjoint** [claim](#stmt-j-adjoint) / [proof](#j:canonical-functional): Identify the full interior adjoint with stationarity of the quotient constraint functional, eliminate canonical momenta and recover the stationary Einstein–wave-map action. -> The stationary quotient solving the full reduced equations with the original slice tensor and map velocities.
- **step-global-static-form** [claim](#stmt-k-timelike) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-timelike): Use vanishing quotient twist, positive boundary and end collars, and a planar null-set flow argument to exclude interior degeneracy; integrate the closed time-shift form. -> Strict interior timelikeness, a global static time graph and harmonic axial lapse product.
- **step-static-boundary** [claim](#stmt-k-static-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-static-boundary): Divide the boundary Taylor expansions in the zero-lapse case and use the square-root Killing norm with reflection in the positive-lapse case. -> A smooth static inner boundary, tangential metric matching and a controlled logarithmic time term.
- **step-determine-rod-scale** [claim](#stmt-k-rod-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-rod-scale): Uniformize the reflected static meridian, compare the harmonic lapse product on finite strips and integrate its boundary derivative to match the original area. -> Global static isothermal coordinates with model-matched scale and boundary density.
- **step-identify-model** [claim](#stmt-k-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-identification): Apply joint convexity to squared target distance, remove finite strip boundaries using logarithmic capacity cutoffs and determine the remaining harmonic metric factor. -> Equality of the stationary map, static horizontal metric and model lapse.
- **step-original-data-lift** [claim](#stmt-k-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-lift): Integrate the closed angular correction, pull back the model fiber form and use spatial and normal potential identities to recover every tensor and field component. -> A well-defined original-data embedding on the regular interior extending across ordinary axes and approaching infinity.
- **step-horizon-extension** [claim](#stmt-k-horizon) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-horizon): Use co-rotating angular variables and smooth null coordinates to cancel the logarithmic time singularity, then establish spacelikeness, injectivity and properness. -> A smooth spacelike embedding with a smooth future normal and the stated future-horizon or bifurcation boundary image.
- **step-embedded-end-identification** [claim](#stmt-k-end-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-end-identification): Restrict the ambient stationary Killing field to the slice, eliminate its spatial translation, prove a single graph by covering arguments and compare mass, charge and angular fluxes. -> Identification of intrinsic and ambient conserved parameter magnitudes and a decaying stationary graph gradient.
- **step-horizon-contact-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-k-horizon-cut-dichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-horizon-cut-dichotomy): Write the horizon section as a nonnegative generator graph, compute its linear transverse null expansion and use outer area minimization and the strong minimum principle. -> The section lies wholly on the open future horizon or is the bifurcation sphere.
- **step-main-theorem-converse-sharpness** [claim](#stmt-m-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-horizon-cut-dichotomy-2): Combine the numerical inequality with stationary identification and the original-data lift, then use horizon-area and end-parameter identities for the converse and verify canonical slices against every boundary hypothesis. -> The closed-branch numerical theorem and strict-branch original-data rigidity, converse and sharpness.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-physical-branch-mass-bound**: The ADM mass satisfies the sharp Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality under the complete exterior, symmetry, electrovacuum, asymptotic, horizon, enclosing-area, and physical-area-branch hypotheses.
- **invariant-corrected-angular-flux**: The conserved angular momentum includes the electromagnetic surface contribution. Its value agrees with the ADM angular momentum at infinity in the prescribed decay class.
- **invariant-polarized-geometric-data**: Polarization preserves ADM energy and preserves area and future expansion for invariant surfaces, while retaining the removed electromagnetic and twist information in the source terms.
- **invariant-prescribed-capacity-cut**: The conformal-cut result converts a lower bound on every meridional crossing length into a pointwise boundary density bound while allowing the exterior conformal capacity to be prescribed independently.
- **invariant-deformation-limit-order**: The deformation gives curvature and energy control after exhaustion at each fixed deformation parameter. The final passage uses numerical energy inequalities and requires no convergence of the deformation metrics.
- **invariant-local-axial-regularity**: The local gradient and Hessian estimates require a positive ellipticity lower bound and bounded gradient and forcing. They use only measurable bounds on the axial coefficient and forcing.
- **invariant-original-data-rigidity**: Equality on the strict physical area branch identifies the original metric, second fundamental form, and electromagnetic fields with an admissible spacelike exterior slice of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime. The embedding extends smoothly to the boundary with a future unit normal.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-bare-boundary-angular-momentum**: Insert the bare gravitational horizon surface integral as the conserved angular momentum. -> The electromagnetic contribution can change the horizon flux even though the corrected total flux agrees with the ADM angular momentum at infinity.
- **anti-use-unrestricted-electromagnetic-tails**: Apply the mass bound to electromagnetic fields with arbitrary angular leading tails. -> Such fields need not satisfy the Coulomb asymptotics that support the primitive decay and disappearance of the electromagnetic surface term at infinity.
- **anti-use-below-physical-area-branch**: Use the sharp mass expression as a proved bound below the physical area branch. -> The area branch is explicitly assumed; the argument does not derive it for arbitrary input data.
- **anti-use-extremal-rigidity**: Classify equality at the extremal endpoint using the subextremal embedding conclusion. -> The numerical inequality includes the endpoint, but the equality classification is restricted to the strict branch.
- **anti-use-unchecked-enclosing-area**: Treat a locally detected marginally outer trapped surface as sufficient input for the area bound. -> The hypotheses also require outermostness and area minimization over the full enclosing-cut class, including nonsymmetric cuts and all boundary components.
- **anti-use-uniform-deformation-regularity**: Use the fixed-parameter classical estimates as uniform compactness estimates along the deformation sequence. -> The regularity constants may depend arbitrarily on the fixed deformation parameters and are explicitly not uniform along the final parameter limit.
- **anti-use-finite-numerical-certificate**: Certify the geometric theorem by evaluating the mass formula on finitely many parameter choices or approximate initial data. -> Algebraic agreement does not establish the global geometric hypotheses, existence of the coupled deformation, or the original-data rigidity implication.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a continuum geometric inequality and an original-data rigidity theorem, with no finite constructive decision procedure supplied for its global hypotheses or conclusions. The deformation existence statement explicitly uses an unbounded-domain limit: “It is the local smooth limit of solutions on expanding truncations.” The equality argument also uses functional-analytic separation: “Separation of an open convex cone from a linear subspace gives a nonzero continuous positive functional on $\mathcal X$ annihilating $L(V)$.” Finite checks of the explicit model parameters would address only algebraic subidentities and would not certify the exterior theorem, the deformation existence argument, or the smooth rigidity reconstruction.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exterior deformation is established through smooth solutions and exhaustion. The paper supplies neither a software implementation nor a discretization with certified error control. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicit Kerr–Newman metric and Maxwell potential provide an analytical background for black-hole simulation benchmarks. Construct the comparison parameters from an admissible model radius, evaluate the canonical slice and electromagnetic field, and use the stated conserved quantities and horizon area as reference values. Adapting this continuous construction to a simulation requires separate numerical error control. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conformal-cut construction proves existence of an exterior holomorphic map with prescribed capacity, but supplies no finite representation or certified computational procedure for producing that map from an arbitrary continuous weight. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coupled deformation uses a compact map, topological degree and exhaustion. These arguments do not specify a terminating algorithm, computable stopping criterion or complexity bound for the general exterior problem. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The target geometry and its unique geodesics organize continuous potential maps. They do not define a discrete storage representation, update operation or data-structure invariant. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The regularity estimates concern smooth solutions with fixed deformation parameters. They provide no accelerator architecture, parallel execution scheme or hardware resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gauge independence and conserved angular flux are geometric properties of electrovacuum fields. They supply no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Identification of the stationary map follows from geometric uniqueness under equality assumptions. The paper gives no learned model, training objective or statistical generalization result. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The minimum-area envelope is a variational result over geometric enclosing sets. Compactness and attainment do not provide a finite operational optimization model or an implementable enclosure optimizer. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The multiplier identity concerns measures on geometric variation spaces. It defines neither communicating agents nor a distributed agreement or fault-tolerance protocol. |
| other (General relativity and geometric analysis benchmarks) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Canonical Kerr–Newman exterior slices give explicit sharp examples against which admissible initial-data calculations can be compared. Construct the canonical metric, tensor and electromagnetic fields from the model parameters, then compare their conserved fluxes and horizon area with the sharp mass bound. The theorem supplies an analytical consistency condition; validating its global hypotheses remains a separate task. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-exterior-topology-and-completeness** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires the specified connected exterior topology, a compact spherical boundary, smooth data up to that boundary, completeness with the boundary included, and a single asymptotically flat end. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem to multiple asymptotic ends, disconnected exteriors, cylindrical inner ends, or a different boundary topology.']
- **limit-exact-axial-symmetry** (hypothesis): The rotational action must preserve the metric, second fundamental form, electromagnetic fields and boundary. Approximate symmetry does not establish this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Apply the axial reduction or main theorem to generic nonsymmetric initial data.']
- **limit-source-free-electrovacuum** (hypothesis): The original-data theorem assumes source-free electrovacuum constraints. Its enlarged sourced comparison has a separate pointwise residual inequality and regularity contract. Blocked use: ['Apply the original-data rigidity conclusion to arbitrary matter or charged material sources.', 'Replace the sourced residual inequality by the dominant energy condition alone.']
- **limit-coulomb-decay-and-rest-frame** (hypothesis): The theorem uses the prescribed differentiated metric, tensor and Coulomb electromagnetic asymptotics, finite conserved fluxes, positive ADM energy and vanishing ADM momentum. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to boosted data without a separate argument.', 'Allow arbitrary angular leading electromagnetic tails while retaining the same conclusion.']
- **limit-corrected-angular-momentum** (formal_scope): The conserved angular momentum on finite enclosing surfaces includes the electromagnetic correction. The bare gravitational boundary integral need not equal the angular momentum at infinity. Blocked use: ['Insert the bare gravitational horizon integral as the conserved total angular momentum.']
- **limit-global-horizon-conditions** (hypothesis): A boundary with vanishing outgoing expansion is insufficient by itself. The theorem also requires the stated global outermostness condition and area minimization over every enclosing cut. Blocked use: ['Treat a locally detected marginally outer trapped surface as a complete admissibility certificate.', 'Verify the area hypothesis using only a sampled family of invariant surfaces.']
- **limit-full-cut-boundary-counting** (formal_scope): An enclosing cut counts its full intrinsic boundary, including all components and portions coinciding with the original boundary. Neither symmetry nor trapping is required of a cut. Blocked use: ['Discard additional boundary components when comparing enclosing areas.', 'Restrict the main area-minimizing hypothesis to trapped or invariant cuts.']
- **limit-physical-area-branch** (hypothesis): The physical area branch is an explicit assumption. The paper does not obtain it from an independent area-charge-angular-momentum theorem. Blocked use: ['Use the main mass bound below the assumed physical area branch.', 'Present the branch condition as a conclusion of the main proof.']
- **limit-extremal-and-interior-rigidity** (edge_case): The numerical inequality includes the closed branch, but equality classification is asserted only on its strict branch. The classification concerns the exterior and does not extend behind the boundary. Blocked use: ['Classify extremal equality data by the subextremal embedding conclusion.', 'Identify an extremal cylindrical end with the compact boundary.', 'Infer an interior classification behind the horizon.']
- **limit-local-quotient-reconstruction** (formal_scope): The converse reconstruction from the reduced Einstein–wave-map equations is local and subject to angular and electromagnetic gauge. Global embedding requires the later topology, identification and horizon-extension arguments. Blocked use: ['Infer a global spacetime embedding from the local quotient reconstruction alone.', 'Discard normal map velocities when recovering the original tensor and fields.']
- **limit-conformal-cut-weight-contract** (hypothesis): The conformal-cut construction requires the stated positive continuous quotient weight, uniform end behavior, controlled puncture profiles and a lower length bound for every crossing. Its boundary density uses a strictly smaller lower bound, and construction constants may depend on capacity. Blocked use: ['Infer the pointwise cut density from an arbitrary length weight.', 'Replace the universal crossing bound with checks on selected paths.', 'Assume estimates uniform over all prescribed capacities.']
- **limit-analytic-cut-existence** (nonconstructive): The exterior analytic boundary problem is solved by continuation, compactness and closedness. The supplied proof does not give a finite terminating procedure for arbitrary admissible weights. Blocked use: ['Treat the conformal-cut existence argument as a certified numerical conformal-mapping algorithm.']
- **limit-riemannian-comparison-boundary-and-sources** (hypothesis): The Riemannian comparison requires inherited normalized potentials, controlled asymptotic flatness, the scalar source inequality, an admissible inner boundary and a crossing-length lower bound. Strict scalar positivity can be omitted only under the stated stable minimal sphere exception. Blocked use: ['Apply the comparison to an arbitrary asymptotically flat metric with no source or inner-boundary checks.', 'Omit strict scalar positivity without the specified boundary exception.']
- **limit-strict-preparation-before-deformation** (hypothesis): The neutral deformation theorem applies to strictly prepared polarized data, including a strictly future trapped boundary, compactly supported tensor, smooth end symbol bounds and a positive constraint margin with compact and end lower bounds. Blocked use: ['Apply the neutral deformation theorem directly to merely marginal boundary data without preparation.', 'Remove the positive margin or tensor cutoff from the deformation hypotheses.']
- **limit-selected-full-regions-and-collars** (formal_scope): The reduced domain is selected using full trapped regions and fixed stable collars before the deformation parameters are chosen. Height separation depends on disjointness from the corresponding selected full region. Blocked use: ['Replace the full trapped regions by arbitrary detected components.', 'Change the reduced domain or collars during the final deformation-parameter limit.']
- **limit-homotopy-stage-contracts** (formal_scope): The homotopy estimates apply to the prescribed sequence of equations, assigned heights, collar corrections and simultaneous source and boundary scaling. Strict height monotonicity is retained in every stage. Blocked use: ['Transfer the floor exclusion or collar estimates to an arbitrary interpolation of the coupled equations.', 'Alter the divergence source independently of its prescribed boundary scaling at the final stage.']
- **limit-floor-coefficient-order** (hypothesis): Floor exclusion requires choosing the small source coefficient before the sufficiently large polynomial penalty coefficient, and applies on sufficiently large finite truncations to solutions on the allowed side of the floor. Blocked use: ['Treat arbitrary penalty coefficients as satisfying the floor-exclusion theorem.', 'Assume floor exclusion for solutions already lying below the allowed floor.']
- **limit-stage-specific-collar-traces** (formal_scope): The signed lower collar-slope bounds and weighted trace estimates concern the initial homotopy stages. The later collar statement provides an upper slope bound rather than the same signed lower bounds. Blocked use: ['Use the initial signed slope bounds throughout every homotopy stage.', 'Apply the weighted trace lemma without its stage and collar hypotheses.']
- **limit-local-gradient-ellipticity-and-boundary** (hypothesis): The local gradient theorem is an a priori result for smooth solutions with controlled patch geometry, a positive ellipticity lower bound, bounded gradient and forcing, and constant data on any boundary face. Measurability of the coefficient does not remove these conditions. Blocked use: ['Apply the estimate when the ellipticity lower bound degenerates.', 'Infer existence of rough solutions from the a priori estimate alone.', 'Use the boundary estimate with arbitrary nonconstant boundary data.']
- **limit-fixed-parameter-regularity** (approximation): The classical bootstrap fixes the prepared data, domain, collars and deformation parameters. Its constants may depend arbitrarily on those fixed parameters and are not asserted to remain uniform in the final deformation limit. Blocked use: ['Extract a smooth limiting deformation metric using these estimates alone.', 'Turn the qualitative regularity estimates into a discretization error or complexity guarantee.']
- **limit-trace-solve-versus-physical-solution** (formal_scope): The separate scalar trace solve accepts invariant input with the prescribed regularity and zero outer value, without a floor assumption. This scalar solvability does not itself solve the coupled physical system. Blocked use: ['Impose the physical floor as an unstated prerequisite of the separate trace solve.', 'Identify a solution of the scalar trace equation with a solution of the coupled deformation equations.']
- **limit-degree-and-exhaustion-existence** (nonconstructive): Physical existence follows from compact-map degree and a local smooth exhaustion limit. This does not supply a finite solver, certified stopping criterion or general numerical error bound. Blocked use: ['Certify the deformation existence theorem from a finite collection of numerical solutions.', 'Infer convergence of an arbitrary numerical iteration.']
- **limit-energy-flux-order-of-limits** (formal_scope): The energy estimate first exhausts the outer truncations at fixed deformation parameter and then takes the parameter limit with the prepared data and prescribed loss fixed. Only numerical energy inequalities are passed to that limit. Blocked use: ['Interchange truncation exhaustion and the deformation-parameter limit without additional estimates.', 'Infer convergence of the deformation metrics from the vanishing negative flux.']
- **limit-equality-multipliers** (nonconstructive): The stationary adjoint argument assumes exact equality on the strict branch and uses positive functional separation and measure representation. Near equality does not establish the stationary adjoint or its exact rigidity conclusion. Blocked use: ['Infer a quantitative stability estimate from approximate saturation of the mass bound.', 'Treat the multiplier measures as outputs of a supplied finite construction.']
- **limit-minimum-area-envelope** (formal_scope): The minimum-area functional uses a compact family of minimizers and a one-sided envelope derivative. Uniqueness of the minimizing enclosure is not required or established by this argument. Blocked use: ['Differentiate the area functional by selecting an arbitrary minimizer.', 'Assume a unique minimizing enclosure from attainment alone.']
- **limit-original-data-lift-and-horizon** (formal_scope): Recovering the original data requires the angular correction, all spatial and normal potential identities and inverse duality rotation. Smooth horizon attachment uses the boundary lapse alternatives; the converse retains every theorem hypothesis on the slice. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the sharp Kerr–Newman Penrose inequality

$`\displaystyle m^2\ge \frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2} +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.`$

Here $`Q^2=Q_e^2+Q_b^2`$, and <i>J</i> is the conserved total angular momentum, including its electromagnetic contribution. The result applies to smooth axisymmetric electrovacuum exteriors in the stated one-ended topological and decay class, with a connected outermost, outer-area-minimizing future marginally outer trapped boundary, Coulomb electromagnetic asymptotics, zero ADM momentum, and the explicitly assumed physical-area condition $`A\ge4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}`$. No maximality assumption is made. On the strict area branch, equality within this class characterizes the original data as an admissible spacelike exterior slice of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime, with the boundary mapped smoothly to a future-horizon cross-section or the bifurcation sphere.

<a id="m:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a black-hole exterior, the Penrose inequality compares total mass with the area of the horizon. Electric and magnetic charge and angular momentum sharpen the expected lower bound to the mass of the Kerr–Newman black hole with the same horizon area and conserved quantities. The geometric problem is to obtain this comparison directly from initial data, without assuming that the data are stationary or that their second fundamental form has zero trace.

Penrose’s proposal relates the mass at spatial infinity to the area required to enclose a black-hole region, as a consequence expected from gravitational collapse and cosmic censorship (Penrose 1973) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Penrose1973](#ref-Penrose1973)]. In the time-symmetric setting, Huisken and Ilmanen proved the Riemannian inequality for a connected horizon by weak inverse mean curvature flow (Huisken and Ilmanen 2001) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HuiskenIlmanen2001](#ref-HuiskenIlmanen2001)]; Bray’s conformal flow established the inequality using the total area of an outermost minimal boundary with several components (Bray 2001) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Bray2001](#ref-Bray2001)]. For charged Riemannian data, Khuri, Weinstein and Yamada proved the sharp inequality with multiple horizon components under the appropriate area branch condition, together with an upper bound for the area radius without that condition (Khuri, Weinstein, and Yamada 2017) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[KhuriWeinsteinYamada2017](#ref-KhuriWeinsteinYamada2017)].

Angular momentum introduces additional boundary and gauge information. Dain, Khuri, Weinstein and Yamada established mass–charge–angular-momentum and lower-area bounds for simply connected, axisymmetric maximal data with two ends (Dain et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DainKhuriWeinsteinYamada2013](#ref-DainKhuriWeinsteinYamada2013)]. Khuri, Sokolowsky and Weinstein obtained a Penrose-type bound for maximal axisymmetric data with a connected minimal boundary; their comparison retains an integral of the horizon data and gives the Kerr–Newman expression under additional horizon matching conditions (Khuri, Sokolowsky, and Weinstein 2019) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[KhuriSokolowskyWeinstein2019](#ref-KhuriSokolowskyWeinstein2019)]. Their harmonic-map method is a useful starting point for the comparison developed here.

The deformation and first-variation methods draw on the manuscript *Equality and rigidity in the spacetime Penrose inequality* (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[NeutralExterior2026](#ref-NeutralExterior2026)]. The related charged companion (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ConditionalCharged2026](#ref-ConditionalCharged2026)] incorporates a numerical theorem whose earlier formulation assumed both a neutral exterior inequality and a separate elliptic existence input; the companion proves those inputs internally. We establish the deformation and equality arguments needed here at their stated scope; no earlier neutral, charged or rotating Penrose inequality is assumed as an input.

We prove the connected-horizon, outer-area-minimizing, rest-frame formulation of the axisymmetric Kerr–Newman Penrose conjecture: $$m^2\ge\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2}
                 +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}{A}.$$ Here $A$ is the horizon area, $Q^2=Q_e^2+Q_b^2$, and $J$ is the conserved total angular momentum, including the electromagnetic contribution. Theorem <a href="#m:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:main">2</a> states the precise geometric and asymptotic hypotheses and the nondegenerate equality classification. The result permits both monopole charges, all angular momenta on the physical area branch, and arbitrary second fundamental forms satisfying the constraints.

The proof has three main ingredients. First, a conformal-cut lemma converts a lower bound for all meridional crossing lengths into a pointwise boundary density bound at any prescribed exterior conformal capacity. This removes the need to prescribe the horizon rod of a comparison model. Second, a neutral two-function deformation transfers the full electromagnetic and twist source inequality to a Riemannian comparison metric. We prove its barriers, estimates, regularity, existence and mass-flux bound; the construction requires no previous elliptic existence hypothesis. The potentials are kept fixed, and a rowwise square completion carries their information through the deformation. Third, equality produces a causal stationary adjoint for the sourced constraints. The quotient equations determine the horizon scale and the Kerr–Newman map, after which a smooth spacetime lift recovers every component of the original data. This last step retains arbitrary admissible time graphs.

The conformal-cut argument and the local gradient estimate used in the deformation are independent analytic tools. The equality argument also avoids a compactness theorem for a sequence of deformation metrics: only numerical inequalities are passed to the deformation limit, and stationarity is obtained separately by first variations at the original data.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Plan of the proof.

Section <a href="#a:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:section">3</a> develops the axial reduction and the model. Sections <a href="#c:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:section">4</a> and <a href="#r:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="r:section">5</a> prove the cut lemma and the Riemannian comparison. Section <a href="#d:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:reduction">6</a> reduces the numerical theorem to a deformation property, proved in Section <a href="#d:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:proof">7</a>. Section <a href="#j:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="j:section">8</a> constructs the stationary adjoint at equality, and Section <a href="#k:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="k:section">9</a> identifies and lifts the original slice. Methodological connections and the standard external tools are cited where they enter.

<a id="m:setting"></a>
### Geometric setting and main theorem

We work in three spatial dimensions, with $G=c=1$ and zero cosmological constant. The sign and asymptotic conventions below are fixed throughout.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Exterior data and constraints

Let $\Omega$ be a smooth connected oriented manifold diffeomorphic to $\mathbb R^3$ minus an open ball. Its nonempty compact boundary $S$ is a two-sphere. Let $g$ be a smooth Riemannian metric, complete as a metric space with $S$ included, and let $K$ be a smooth symmetric covariant two-tensor. The electric and magnetic fields $E_{\mathrm f},B_{\mathrm f}$ are smooth vector fields. All data are smooth up to $S$, and there is one asymptotically flat end.

Assume a smooth effective $U(1)$ action preserves $g,K,E_{\mathrm f},B_{\mathrm f}$ and $S$. Its Killing generator $\eta$ has period $2\pi$; in the asymptotic chart it is the usual oriented rotation about the $x^3$-axis. No stationarity is assumed.

We use the future-normal convention $$K(X,Y)=\overline g(\overline\nabla_X n,Y),\qquad n\text{ future unit normal}.$$ Writing $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_gK$, define the constraint densities by <a id="m:densities"></a>$$\label{m:densities}
 16\pi\mu=R_g+\tau^2-\abs{K}_g^2,\qquad
 8\pi(J_{\mathrm{con}})_i=\nabla^j(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij}).$$ The data are source-free electrovacuum: <a id="m:electrovac"></a>$$\label{m:electrovac}
 \mu=\frac{\abs{E_{\mathrm f}}_g^2+\abs{B_{\mathrm f}}_g^2}{8\pi},
 \qquad J_{\mathrm{con}}=\frac{(E_{\mathrm f}\mathbin\times B_{\mathrm f})^\flat}{4\pi},
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_gE_{\mathrm f}=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_gB_{\mathrm f}=0.$$ Here the cross product and Hodge star use the given spatial orientation, and $\flat$ is metric duality. The covector $J_{\mathrm{con}}$ is a local momentum density; it is distinct from the total angular momentum $J$. In a spacetime realization with electromagnetic two-form $F$, the induced fields have the conventions <a id="m:field-convention"></a>$$\label{m:field-convention}
 E_{\mathrm f}^{\flat}=(\iota_nF)|_{T\Omega},\qquad
 B_{\mathrm f}^{\flat}=\star_g(F|_{T\Omega}).$$

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Asymptotics and conserved quantities

In asymptotically Euclidean coordinates $x$, put $r=\abs{x}$ and assume <a id="m:asymptotics"></a>$$\label{m:asymptotics}
 \begin{split}
 g_{ij}-\delta_{ij}&=O_4(r^{-1}),\qquad K_{ij}=O_3(r^{-3}),\\
 E_{\mathrm f}^i&=Q_e\frac{x^i}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-3}),\qquad
 B_{\mathrm f}^i=Q_b\frac{x^i}{r^3}+O_3(r^{-3}).
 \end{split}$$ For $O_k(r^{-a})$, every coordinate derivative of order $j\le k$ is $O(r^{-a-j})$. Thus the fields have Coulomb leading terms; higher multipoles are allowed. Require $\mu$ and $\abs{J_{\mathrm{con}}}_g$ to be integrable and the following limits to exist and be finite. On a coordinate sphere $S_R$, let $n_\delta,\mathrm dA_\delta$ denote the Euclidean outward normal and area, and $\nu_R,\mathrm dA_g$ their metric counterparts: <a id="m:energy"></a><a id="m:momentum"></a><a id="m:angular-adm"></a><a id="m:charges"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 E_{\mathrm{ADM}}&=\frac1{16\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty}
  \int_{S_R}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj})n_\delta^i\,\mathrm dA_\delta,
  \label{m:energy}\\
 (P_{\mathrm{ADM}})_i&=\frac1{8\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty}
  \int_{S_R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})n_\delta^j\,\mathrm dA_\delta,
  \label{m:momentum}\\
 J_{\mathrm{ADM}}&=\frac1{8\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty}
  \int_{S_R}(K_{ij}-\tau g_{ij})\nu_R^i\eta^j\,\mathrm dA_g,
  \label{m:angular-adm}\\
 (Q_e,Q_b)&=\frac1{4\pi}\lim_{R\to\infty}
  \int_{S_R}\bigl(g(E_{\mathrm f},\nu_R),g(B_{\mathrm f},\nu_R)\bigr)\,\mathrm dA_g.
  \label{m:charges}
\end{aligned}$$ Assume $P_{\mathrm{ADM}}=0$ and $E_{\mathrm{ADM}}>0$, and set <a id="m:mass-charge"></a>$$\label{m:mass-charge}
 m=E_{\mathrm{ADM}}=\sqrt{E_{\mathrm{ADM}}^2-\abs{P_{\mathrm{ADM}}}^2},
 \qquad Q=\sqrt{Q_e^2+Q_b^2}.$$ The imposed decay of $K$ is consistent with this rest-frame condition.

<a id="m:magnetic-primitive"></a>
For $Q>0$, use the constant duality rotation <a id="m:duality"></a>$$\label{m:duality}
 E'=\frac{Q_eE_{\mathrm f}+Q_bB_{\mathrm f}}Q,\qquad
 B'=\frac{-Q_bE_{\mathrm f}+Q_eB_{\mathrm f}}Q.$$ For $Q=0$, set $E'=E_{\mathrm f}$, $B'=B_{\mathrm f}$. The rotated charges are $(Q,0)$, and $B'=O_3(r^{-3})$. The zero flux removes the $H^2(\Omega)$ obstruction to a smooth invariant one-form $\alpha$ satisfying $$\label{m:magnetic-primitive}
 \mathrm d\alpha=\star_g(B'^\flat),\qquad \alpha=O_2(r^{-2})$$ in asymptotic Cartesian components. Its construction and normalizations are given in Section <a href="#a:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:section">3</a>; no primitive of the original magnetic field is assumed when $Q_b\ne0$.

For an invariant enclosing surface $\Sigma$, with normal toward infinity, define the total angular momentum <a id="m:total-angular"></a>$$\label{m:total-angular}
 J_{\mathrm{tot}}(\Sigma)=\frac1{8\pi}\int_\Sigma(K-\tau g)(\nu,\eta)\,\mathrm dA_g
  +\frac1{4\pi}\int_\Sigma\alpha(\eta)g(E',\nu)\,\mathrm dA_g.$$ Cartan’s identity and the constraints give $$(E'\mathbin\times B')\cdot\eta=-E'\cdot\nabla(\alpha(\eta)),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g\bigl((K-\tau g)(\eta,\cdot)^\sharp+2\alpha(\eta)E'\bigr)=0.$$ Consequently $J_{\mathrm{tot}}$ is conserved between such surfaces and is unchanged by smooth invariant gauge changes. The electromagnetic surface term vanishes at infinity by Equation <a href="#m:magnetic-primitive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:magnetic-primitive">[m:magnetic-primitive]</a>, so <a id="m:J"></a>$$\label{m:J}
 J:=J_{\mathrm{tot}}(S)=\lim_{R\to\infty}J_{\mathrm{tot}}(S_R)=J_{\mathrm{ADM}}.$$ This identity concerns the corrected total flux. The bare gravitational surface integral on $S$ can differ from its value at infinity.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The horizon and enclosing area

On $S$, let $\nu$ point toward the end, and put <a id="m:expansion"></a>$$\label{m:expansion}
 H_S=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_S\nu,\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_SK=(g^{ij}-\nu^i\nu^j)K_{ij},\qquad
 \theta_+(S)=H_S+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_SK.$$ Assume $\theta_+(S)=0$. Also assume that no compact smooth embedded enclosing surface lying entirely in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$, possibly disconnected, satisfies $\theta_+\le0$ everywhere with its normal toward infinity. This is the outermostness condition used below; no condition is imposed on the inward null expansion.

<a id="stmt-m-cut"></a>
<div id="m:cut" class="definition">

**Definition 1** (Enclosing cut). An enclosing cut $\Gamma$ is the full compact intrinsic boundary of a connected smooth codimension-zero submanifold $D\subset\Omega$, closed as a subset of $\Omega$, whose interior lies in $\operatorname{int}\Omega$ and which contains the entire sufficiently distant end. The boundary is smooth, embedded and two-sided. All its components are counted, including any portion coinciding with $S$. No symmetry or trapping condition is imposed on $\Gamma$.

</div>

<a id="m:branch"></a>
Assume that $S$ is outer area-minimizing in the precise sense <a id="m:area"></a>$$\label{m:area}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(\Gamma)\ge A:=\mathop{\mathrm{Area}}_g(S)>0
 \quad\text{for every enclosing cut }\Gamma.$$ Taking $D=\Omega$ gives the allowed cut $S$, so $A$ is exactly the minimum enclosing area. Finally assume the physical area branch $$\label{m:branch}
 A\ge4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}.$$ We use this branch as a hypothesis; no area-charge-angular-momentum theorem is needed to deduce it.

<a id="stmt-m-main"></a>
<div id="m:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Penrose inequality and nondegenerate rigidity). *Under the hypotheses of this section, <a id="m:penrose"></a>$$\label{m:penrose}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 m^2\ge\frac{A}{16\pi}+\frac{Q^2}{2}
           +\frac{\pi(Q^4+4J^2)}A.}$$ If $A>4\pi\sqrt{Q^4+4J^2}$ and equality holds, the original exterior $(\Omega,g,K,E_{\mathrm f},B_{\mathrm f})$ admits a smooth spacelike embedding into the domain of outer communication together with the appropriate future horizon of a subextremal dyonic Kerr–Newman spacetime with mass $m$, angular momentum $J$, and charges $Q_e,Q_b$, in the conventions above. The induced metric, second fundamental form with a consistent future normal, and induced fields agree with the given data. The end approaches the corresponding spatial infinity. The embedding and normal extend smoothly to $S$, which maps to a smooth future-horizon cross-section or to the bifurcation sphere.*

*Conversely, every such Kerr–Newman exterior slice satisfying all the hypotheses and meeting that horizon attains equality. Nonconstant time graphs and nonzero second fundamental forms are permitted.*

</div>

The closed branch in Equation <a href="#m:branch" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:branch">[m:branch]</a> is included in the numerical inequality. No equality classification at its extremal endpoint is asserted. In particular, an extremal cylindrical end is not identified with the compact boundary in the hypotheses. The theorem concerns only the exterior and gives no classification behind $S$.

For clarity, the proof first establishes the numerical inequality, including the endpoint, in Corollary <a href="#d:numerical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:numerical">19</a>. The proof of its deformation input is completed in Section <a href="#d:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:proof">7</a>. The equality implication and converse are completed in Section <a href="#k:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="k:section">9</a>.

<a id="a:section"></a>
### Axial variables and the comparison family

We first encode the fields and the components removed by polarization in three scalar potentials. The metric and second fundamental form in this section are the original data until a subscript $0$ is introduced. Throughout, $\eta$ is the prescribed generator of period $2\pi$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The orbit space and its smooth angular coordinate

<a id="stmt-a-topology"></a>
<div id="a:topology" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Orbit topology). *The quotient of $\Omega$ by the circle action is a half annulus: its boundary consists of the compact image of $S$ and two noncompact axis faces, each joining an endpoint of that image to infinity. There are no exceptional orbits. The regular part of $\Omega$ is a trivial principal circle bundle over the interior of this quotient. It admits a global angular coordinate that agrees with the prescribed azimuth on a smaller asymptotic end and with smooth polar gauges near the axes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-topology"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-topology</summary>

*Proof.* Truncate the given end at a sufficiently large rotation sphere. Both boundary spheres carry ordinary effective rotations. Here is a direct verification of the assertion for an invariant sphere. Its Euler characteristic forces fixed points. At a fixed point the tangent representation is a planar rotation with integer weight. That weight has absolute value one: otherwise a nonidentity group element would fix the point and have identity differential both on the sphere and on its invariant inward normal, hence would be an isometry with identity first jet and therefore the identity on the connected ambient manifold. At a nonfixed point a finite stabilizer acts trivially on the orbit tangent. It acts trivially also on the transverse tangent line, by orientation, and on the inward normal. The same first-jet argument excludes such a stabilizer. The slice theorem now makes the quotient of the sphere an interval, with an ordinary rotation disk at either endpoint; the free circle bundle over its interior is a product. Consequently the action extends over an ordinary rotation ball.

Cap the two boundary spheres equivariantly, using invariant collars. The resulting closed three-manifold is simply connected. Its orbit space is a compact orientable surface with nonempty boundary, the boundary arising from fixed axes. The only other possible singular orbits are isolated exceptional fibers, represented by interior cone points of finite cyclic order. The orbifold fundamental group of this surface is a quotient of the fundamental group of the three-manifold. Indeed, away from axes and exceptional fibers, loops lift to paths; an endpoint displacement in a fiber can be closed within that fiber without changing the projected loop. Filling an axis tube introduces no nontrivial projected fiber relation. Filling an exceptional solid torus imposes the relation that the corresponding meridian raised to the isotropy order is trivial, as seen in its cyclic disk cover. Van Kampen therefore gives the asserted surjection onto the orbifold group.

An orientable surface with nonempty boundary and cyclic cone points has orbifold group equal to the free product of its ordinary free surface group and the cyclic groups at those points. Triviality of this group implies that the surface is a disk and that there are no cone points. Removing the two cap quotients gives the half-annulus description. Its regular interior is contractible, so its principal circle bundle is trivial.

For the last smoothness assertion, use equivariant normal disks near each axis and a signed transverse polar radius there. Changes of angular reference are smooth functions on the quotient, even in that signed radius. Their local phase lifts can be patched by a partition of unity; the only obstruction is the integral circle cocycle, which vanishes on this orbit rectangle. The same construction relative to a far-end strip preserves the given azimuth there. Thus the product gauge has the stated compatibility with the smooth axes. No isothermal, Weyl, or other metric coordinates have been assumed. ◻

</details>

Off the axis write, with horizontal lifts understood, <a id="a:metric-splitting"></a>$$\label{a:metric-splitting}
 g=h+X^2(\mathrm d\phi+\mathcal A)^2,
 \qquad X=|\eta|_g,\qquad e=X^{-1}\eta.$$ Orient the meridional metric $h$ by $\mathrm dV_g=\mathrm dA_h\wedge X(\mathrm d\phi+\mathcal A)$. Thus $*_h$ sends the first covector of a positive orthonormal meridional coframe to the second. A subscript $H$ denotes a horizontal component, and horizontal vector fields will be identified with their $h$-dual covectors when applying $*_h$.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The conserved potentials and their axis behavior

Perform the constant duality rotation in Theorem <a href="#m:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:main">2</a> and write $E,B$ for the resulting fields in this section. Their charges are $Q,0$. The rotation preserves $|E|^2+|B|^2$ and $E\times B$, so it preserves the electrovacuum constraints. It gives $B=O_3(r^{-3})$ on the end.

<a id="stmt-a-angular-momentum"></a>
<div id="a:angular-momentum" class="lemma">

<a id="a:total-flux"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Magnetic primitive and total angular momentum). *There is a smooth invariant one-form $\alpha$ with $$\mathrm d\alpha=*_g B^\flat,\qquad \alpha=O_2(r^{-2}).$$ The flux $$\label{a:total-flux}
 \frac1{8\pi}\int_\Sigma(K-\tau g)(\nu,\eta)\,\mathrm dA_g
 +\frac1{4\pi}\int_\Sigma\alpha(\eta)g(E,\nu)\,\mathrm dA_g$$ is independent of the invariant enclosing surface $\Sigma$ and of the smooth invariant gauge for $\alpha$. It equals $J_{\rm ADM}$ at infinity, and hence equals the prescribed $J$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-angular-momentum"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-angular-momentum</summary>

*Proof.* The magnetic flux form is closed by $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_gB=0$. Since $\Omega$ retracts onto a sphere, its only two-dimensional de Rham period is the magnetic flux, which is zero after rotation. The form is therefore exact. The decay can be imposed without sacrificing smoothness. On a rescaled annulus, radial homotopy reduces the primitive problem to a two-form on a fixed sphere; its zero integral is exactly the condition for a spherical primitive. These homotopy operators, followed by a fixed spherical elliptic inverse, have uniform bounds through the required derivatives. Scaling back a two-form of Cartesian order $r^{-3}$ gives a primitive of order $r^{-2}$, with its first two derivatives of the corresponding orders. On consecutive overlapping annuli, the difference of the primitives is a closed one-form and hence is the differential of a function. After subtracting a constant, its rescaled derivatives are bounded. Use cutoff multiples of these functions on the overlaps to glue the annular primitives. The annuli can be chosen with bounded overlap, so these corrections retain the bounds. The same argument glues the end primitive to any interior primitive. All ingredients are smooth on each finite annulus; this avoids any demand for uniform bounds on unspecified higher derivatives at infinity. Averaging over the circle makes $\alpha$ invariant and preserves its equation and decay.

Set $\chi=\alpha(\eta)$. Cartan’s formula gives $$\mathrm d\chi=-\iota_\eta(*_gB^\flat),
 \qquad (E\times B)\cdot\eta=-E\cdot\nabla\chi.$$ The contraction of the momentum constraint with the Killing field, together with $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}E=0$, now gives $$\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g\big((K-\tau g)(\eta,\cdot)^\sharp+2\chi E\big)=0.$$ The divergence theorem proves conservation of Equation <a href="#a:total-flux" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:total-flux">[a:total-flux]</a>. If $\alpha$ is changed by a closed invariant one-form $\beta$, then $\mathrm d(\beta(\eta))=-\iota_\eta\mathrm d\beta=0$. The constant $\beta(\eta)$ is zero on the axis, and thus everywhere. Finally $\alpha(\eta)=O(r^{-1})$, while $E\cdot\nu=O(r^{-2})$; the electromagnetic surface term is $O(r^{-1})$ on the end spheres. This proves its disappearance in the limiting flux. The proof does not identify the bare gravitational flux on $S$ with the flux at infinity. ◻

</details>

<a id="a:potentials"></a>
<a id="a:target"></a>
Let $l=K(e,\cdot)|_H$. The global potentials are defined by $$\label{a:potentials}
 \mathrm d\psi=X*_hE_H,\qquad
 \mathrm d\chi=X*_hB_H,\qquad
 \omega:=\mathrm dz+\psi\,\mathrm d\chi-\chi\,\mathrm d\psi=X^2*_h l.$$ For any positive axial radius $X$ define $$\label{a:target}
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal D_X=(X^{-1}\mathrm d\psi,X^{-1}\mathrm d\chi,X^{-2}\omega),
 \qquad u=\log X,\\
 G=\mathrm du^2+e^{-2u}(\mathrm d\psi^2+\mathrm d\chi^2)
       +e^{-4u}(\mathrm dz+\psi\mathrm d\chi-\chi\mathrm d\psi)^2.
 \end{gathered}$$ The last expression is a Riemannian metric on $\mathbb R^4$, with coordinates $(u,\psi,\chi,z)$.

We verify global integrability and the normalizations in Equation <a href="#a:potentials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:potentials">[a:potentials]</a>. For an invariant field the divergence equation is equivalent to the closedness of $X*_hE_H$, and similarly for $B$. The second of these forms is $\mathrm d(\alpha(\eta))$, by Cartan’s identity with the stated orientation. The axial momentum equation gives $$\mathrm d(X^2*_h l)=2\,\mathrm d\psi\wedge\mathrm d\chi,
 \qquad
 \mathrm d\big(X^2*_h l+2\chi\mathrm d\psi\big)=0.$$ Consequently $X^2*_h l+\chi\mathrm d\psi-\psi\mathrm d\chi$ is closed. The meridional rectangle is simply connected, so all three defining equations integrate there.

<a id="a:axis-values"></a>
Orient a crossing arc so that its tangent is the metric dual of $*_h\nu^\flat$, where $\nu$ is the horizontal normal pointing toward infinity. Rotating the arc gives area element $2\pi X\,\mathrm ds_h$. The electric flux divided by $4\pi$ is therefore $\frac12\int\mathrm d\psi$, and the total angular flux is $$\frac14\int(\omega+2\chi\mathrm d\psi)
       =\frac14\int\mathrm d(z+\chi\psi).$$ All potentials are constant on each axis face. Order those faces by this crossing orientation. Since $\chi=\alpha(\eta)=0$ on both axes, additive constants can and will be chosen so that their values are $$\label{a:axis-values}
 (\psi,\chi,z)=(-Q,0,-2J)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(Q,0,2J).$$ The same computation on end-coordinate spheres proves that these are the original flux normalizations.

<a id="stmt-a-axis-regularity"></a>
<div id="a:axis-regularity" class="lemma">

<a id="a:end-regularity"></a>
<a id="a:end-central"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Axis and end regularity). *In a compact ordinary axis tube, let $y$ be signed transverse polar radius and let $x$ be a coordinate along the axis. The functions $$\psi-\psi_{\rm ax},\qquad\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}$$ are smooth even functions of $y$ of order $y^2$, while $$z+\psi_{\rm ax}\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}\psi-z_{\rm ax}$$ is smooth even of order $y^4$. These statements hold with smooth longitudinal derivatives and at the boundary poles in one-sided charts. On the asymptotic end, the potentials are bounded, and near either pole their differences from the adjacent axis values obey $$\label{a:end-regularity}
 \psi-\psi_{\rm ax}=O(\sin^2\vartheta),\qquad
 \chi=O(r^{-1}\sin^2\vartheta),\qquad
 z-z_{\rm ax}=O(\sin^2\vartheta).$$ The corrected central coordinate satisfies the stronger uniform estimate $$\label{a:end-central}
 \widetilde z:=z+\psi_{\rm ax}\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}\psi-z_{\rm ax}
       =O(\sin^4\vartheta).$$ The estimates have the differentiated versions obtained from Equation <a href="#a:potentials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:potentials">[a:potentials]</a>; in particular their rescalings to end annuli have uniform bounds through the orders used below.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-axis-regularity"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-axis-regularity</summary>

*Proof.* An invariant smooth scalar is even in $y$. A smooth invariant horizontal vector field has an odd radial component and an even longitudinal component. Since $X/y$ is smooth positive even, $X*_hE_H$ and $X*_hB_H$ have radial component $O(y)$ and longitudinal component $O(y^2)$ with the corresponding parities. Integration from the axis gives the first two assertions.

Smoothness of an invariant symmetric tensor gives an angular–axis component $l_x=O(y)$ and an angular–radial component $l_y=O(y^2)$. After multiplication by $X^2$ and rotation by $*_h$, $\omega$ has radial component $O(y^3)$ and longitudinal component $O(y^4)$. Put $\delta\psi=\psi-\psi_{\rm ax}$ and $\delta\chi=\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}$. Then $$\mathrm d(z+\psi_{\rm ax}\chi-\chi_{\rm ax}\psi)
       =\omega-\delta\psi\,\mathrm d\chi
                    +\delta\chi\,\mathrm d\psi.$$ The last two terms have the same radial and longitudinal orders as $\omega$. Integration proves the order-four assertion.

For the end estimates, perform the same argument in rescaled annuli. The leading electric field is the radial Coulomb field, so the leading angular derivative of $\psi$ is $Q\sin\vartheta\,\mathrm d\vartheta$. The remainder contributes a uniformly bounded function of order $\sin^2\vartheta$ at a pole.
